# What the Sacrifices and Circumcision in the Old Testament mean. Chapter 2.

11. For without substance no working can exist; therefore they imagined or typified regeneration for themselves through the sacrifices by fire. In these, they formed or imaged the Serpent-Destroyer in the fire, showing how HE would change God's fire of wrath in the soul's fire into a light-fire and a love-fire, and how the enmity would separate itself from the soul; and how the soul should be changed into an angel through Christ's death (where the love of God gave itself up into this fire).

12. By this modeled image or type, they reached toward God with their desire and earnest prayer. God's Word of Grace had also thus modeled and spoken itself into men, and now there was a conjunction between God and man; for human desire went with this image into God, and God's love-desire went into this image of the Serpent-Destroyer. Thus, the desire of man brought this image of the imagination into the sacrifice, and so the sacrifice was kindled with the holy fire.

<margin>Note. And it was not a common fire that they had. If you, Babel, could understand this, it would be well for you, and you would be delivered from the fables in which you follow your course.</margin>

13. *This holy fire consumed their sacrifice through God's imagination and kindling,* to signify how the <margin>Or, animal image.</margin> ^d bestial image of man should be preserved in the fire of God, and the coarseness of the element be consumed. Out of the fire's consuming should go forth the right, true, created, pure, bright spiritual image in *Adam*, which through this great love shall be preserved and brought through into *clarity* by the fire. In this new image, love itself would be the fire of life, so that it might no longer imagine and imprint falsehood.

### Ch. 2. and the Baptism in the N. T.

For without substance no working can exist: therefore they imagined or typified regeneration for themselves with the sacrifices by fire, and formed or imaged the Serpent-Destroyer in the fire; [typifying] how HE would change God’s wrath-fire in the soul into a light- and love-fire; and how the enmity would separate itself from the soul; and how the soul should, through Christ’s death (in which the love of God would give itself up into this wrath-fire), be changed into an angel.

By this modeled image or type, with their desire and earnest prayer, they pressed through the sacrifice to God; and God's Word with grace had thus also, with the covenant, modeled itself in men. And now there was a conjunction between God and man, for the human desire went with this image through the sacrifice in the holy fire into God, and God's love-desire went into this image of the Serpent-Destroyer: For God kindled the sacrifice with the holy fire.

And it was not a common fire that they had in their sacrifices, though they used wood and sacrifices for it: yet their fire was not from a stone and a steel, but from the highest tincture of the paradisiacal ground, from which the fire of life is sprung. If man could understand it, and did not so run on in blindness, it would be well for him, and he would be delivered from Babel and fables.

*This* holy fire consumed their sacrifices through God’s imagination and kindling: and there the introduced human will, which clings to earthliness, is cleansed and in the holy fire purged, and ransomed from sin, upon the future fulfillment. For the ground out of which this holy fire came manifested itself afterwards in man’s life, in the person of Christ.

Thus stood the figure in their sacrifices [to show] how the earthly image of man should be preserved in the fire of God, and how the coarseness of the elements should be consumed, and out of the consuming of the fire should go forth the right, true, created, pure, bright spiritual image in Adam, which in the fire of God’s wrath, through this holy fire of the great love, should be brought into *clarity*: In which new image, the great fiery love would itself be the fire of life, so that it might no more imagine falsehood.

14. With such an imagination and *belief* or faith, the first people before Christ’s time (before he manifested himself in this embodied Grace-Covenant and became man) were apprehended and taken into the Living Word of God. That is, they were taken into that grace wherein their soul entered into divine rest until the *fulfillment*, when Christ completed this process and arose from death. Then he also arose with his life and substance within them—specifically in their inward ground of the heavenly part which had vanished in *Adam*. Thus, they *have* put on Christ in regards to the soul and spiritual substance. They now wait for their body to be raised out of the *limus* <note>original: "limus"; Latin for "slime" or "mud," referring to the dust of the earth from which man was formed</note> of the earth—that is, the Third Principle, or the outspoken, formed, substantial *outward Word*—by his Spirit of the Resurrection at the Last Day. This is also how it is to be understood concerning Christians who *have* put on Christ here in this life.

<margin>Note.</margin>
15. This foundation of the *Holy Fire* began with *Adam* and *Abel*. When *Abel* and *Cain* offered sacrifices, God looked graciously on *Abel’s* sacrifice because the image or type of *Christ* stood within it through Abel's imagination of faith. Therefore, God kindled his sacrifice with the Holy Fire, and it was acceptable before him, for it was a conjunction with the Divine Desire. But God did not look graciously on *Cain’s* sacrifice, for he did not have *such* faith or *belief*. Instead, he stood in the lust and desire of his own perished nature and had imprinted or imagined for himself the kingdom of this world; therefore, the Holy Fire would not kindle in his image or type.

16. For *Cain* stood in the image of the perished *Adam*, as a true figure of *Adam* after the Fall. *Abel* stood in the figure of the New Regeneration—that is, in *Christ’s figure*—signifying how Christ would enter into death with his sacrifice. Thus, the image of the perished *Adam* stood near *Cain*, signifying how Christ had come to seek lost man and to generate him anew through his sacrifice.

17. But as the vanity of men gained the upper hand and their nature became increasingly evil and shameful, this understanding of the Holy Fire was quenched among them until the Flood came upon them and destroyed them. This was a type of Baptism, signifying how the water of Eternal Life (God’s substantial meekness) would drown and quench that false fire—the false life of the soul—and how the soul would grow up out of the Holy Water in the meekness of God into a *New Life* of Light.

# Chapter 2: And what Baptism is in the New Testament

With this faith, the first men before *Christ’s* time—before *Christ* manifested himself in this incorporated Covenant of Grace and became man—were apprehended and taken into the Living Word of God. That is, they were taken into the grace wherein their soul came into divine rest. <margin>Note: This remained the case until Christ fulfilled this type <note>original: "Type" - a prophetic symbol or foreshadowing</note> and arose from death.</margin> Then he also arose and was manifested with his life and substance in them—specifically, on their inward ground of the heavenly part which had vanished in *Adam*. Thus, they "put on *Christ*" in soul and spirit, and they now wait for their body to be formed out of the *limus* <note>original: "Limus" - Latin for slime or mud; here referring to the "dust of the ground" from which man was made</note> of the earth. This refers to the Third Principle <note>In the author's cosmology, the "Third Principle" is the visible, material world</note> of the visible world's property: the formed, outspoken, substantial Word of the Resurrection at the Last Day. This is also how it is to be understood concerning Christians today.

This ground of the *Holy Fire* began with *Adam*, and with *Abel* and *Cain*. When *Abel* and *Cain* sacrificed, God looked graciously on *Abel’s* sacrifice and kindled it with holy fire, *and the sweet smell went up before the LORD*. Because the image or type of *Christ* stood within *Abel's* faith, God kindled his sacrifice with the Holy Fire, and it was acceptable before God; it was a conjunction with the divine desire. But God did not look graciously on *Cain’s* sacrifice, for he did not have *such* faith or belief. Instead, *Cain* stood in the perished Adamic nature of his own lust and desire, and had imprinted or imagined to himself the kingdom of *this world*. Therefore, the Holy Fire would not kindle in his sacrifice.

*Cain* stood as a figure of the perished *Adam* after the Fall, and *Abel* stood as a figure of the New Regeneration. This signified how *Christ* would go into death with his sacrifice and die for man. The image of *Cain* stood nearby to signify how *Christ* had come to seek poor, fallen man and, through his sacrifice, generate him anew.

But as the vanity of men gained the upper hand and their nature became increasingly evil and shameful, this understanding of the Holy Fire was quenched among them until the Flood came and overthrew them. The Flood was a type of *Baptism*, signifying how the water of eternal life—namely, God’s substantial meekness—would drown and quench that false, fiery life of the soul. It showed how the soul would grow up out of the Holy Water in the meekness of God toward a *New Life* of Light.

Now, when the time came that God renewed his covenant with *Abraham* (which He had first established in Paradise), He gave him the figure of *Christ* again through *Circumcision* and the Holy Fire. <margin>With.</margin>

# Ch. 2. *and what the Baptism in the N. T.*

With this faith, the first men before Christ's time (before Christ manifested himself in this incorporated Covenant of Grace and became man) were apprehended and taken into the Living Word of God—namely, into the grace wherein their soul came into divine rest. <note>Note: This continued until Christ fulfilled this type and arose from death; and then he also, with his life and substance in them (namely, on their inward ground of the heavenly part which vanished in Adam) arose and was manifested, and they put on Christ in soul and spirit.</note> And so they now wait for their body out of the *limus* <note>Latin for slime or mud; here referring to the dust of the ground</note> of the earth—namely, the Third Principle <note>The visible, material world</note> of the property of the visible worlds; that is, the formed, outspoken, substantial Word of the resurrection at the Last Day. This is also how it is to be understood concerning Christians.

This ground of the *Holy Fire* began with *Adam*, and with *Abel* and *Cain*. When *Abel* and *Cain* sacrificed, God looked graciously on *Abel's* sacrifice and kindled it with holy fire, *and the sweet smell went up before the LORD*. Because the image or type of Christ in his faith stood within it, God kindled his sacrifice with the holy fire, and it was acceptable before God; for it was a conjunction with the divine desire. But he did not look graciously on *Cain's*; for he did not have *such* faith or belief, but stood in the perished *Adamical* nature of his own lust and desire, and had imprinted or imagined to himself the kingdom of *this world*; and therefore, the *holy fire would not kindle in his sacrifice.*

*Cain* stood in the *figure* of the *perished Adam after the Fall*, and *Abel* stood in the figure of the *New Regeneration*, <note>signifying</note> how Christ would go into death with his sacrifice and die for man. And so the image of *Cain* stood nearby, <note>signifying</note> how Christ had come to seek the poor, fallen man and, with his sacrifice, regenerate him anew.

But as the vanity of men gained the upper hand and their nature became even more evil and shameful, then this understanding of the holy fire was quenched among them, until the flood came upon them and overthrew them. This was a type of *baptism*, <note>signifying</note> how the water of eternal life (namely, God's substantial meekness) would drown and quench that false, fiery life of the soul, and how the soul would grow up out of the holy water in the meekness of God to a *New Life* of light.

24. In this manner was Israel ransomed from sins in a spiritual way through the type of Christ, through the covenant of God, and through the sacrifice and fire. This holy fire afterwards manifested itself in the humanity of *Christ*, and took away the strength from the poison of the *wrath* of God, and turned it into love, and broke open the strong impression of death in the soul-fire, and brought divine love and meekness into it.

Therefore, God sent baptism before this holy kindling and placed it in the first covenant. For when the Word and the power of the holy fire became man and manifested itself in Christ, then the holy Word in the holy fire, through the assumed humanity, spoke within itself into its fellow members according to the humanity.

->*But reason says;*<-
->*What is, or what does the water-baptism signify?*<-
->*What does God work through it?*<-

Answer: As is mentioned above: The soul had so much enraged itself with wrath in its properties that *thereby* it stood in eternal death. In it, **God’s** love-essence was no longer manifest, wherein it might be able to kindle the holy fire of God, namely: the holy life. Therefore, God sent the holy baptism before and set it in the covenant. For when the Word, namely: the power of the holy fire, became man and revealed itself in Christ, then Christ, with the holy fire through the assumed humanity, in-spoke himself into his fellow members, according to humanity.

the meek Love and Grace in the Covenant flowed forth; with this flowing forth of the Divine Love, the Covenant was set and placed in Water-Baptism. For seeing that Man was earthly and elementary, there also had to be an elementary *medium* or means for that purpose, in which the flowing forth of the Divine Love in the Covenant might take hold, so that a human natural substance might be in the middle, into which the *Divine* and also the *Human Imagination* might enter and infuse itself into humanity for a *new essence* or kindler, in which the Holy Fire might kindle itself in the dry soul's-fire.

28. As was done under circumcision and in the sacrifices, there the beastly fat was a medium in which, by such kindling, the human desire through the Covenant went to meet God, and God's imagination went into the Covenant. Thus stood the Holy Fire in the Covenant, opposite to the wrath in the introduction of the human desire in the sacrifice: God’s imagination in his love-fire went into the Covenant, and the human imagination also went through the sacrifice in the fire into the Covenant. For in the fire, God’s *wrath* was encountered, through which the human imagination must go into the Covenant, and so consume the sin and uncleanness in the human desire which pierced through to God. Thus the *love-fire* stood in the Covenant opposite to the human desire, and it took the human desire in the wrath-fire to itself, according to its purity.

29. And if not, then he would devour them in the curse through this wrath-fire, which was an image, type, or shadow signifying how human vanity should and must be purged through God’s wrath-fire. But in that same wrath-fire, the love-fire stood opposite in the Covenant in the sacrifice, as an image of Christ, and quenched the wrath-fire, so that the soul’s desire, with its prayer and *will*, could pierce through this wrath-fire into God.

30. This love-fire, which stood opposite to the wrath in the sacrifice and which the wrath-fire also kindled so that it devoured the sacrifice, is that which—in the limit of the Covenant, in the seed of the woman—awakened itself with heavenly substantiality, namely, with the essential wisdom, and gave itself up into our substance, which had vanished as to God, yet was *heavenly*.

Since man was of an elementary, earthly kind, there also had to be an elementary medium or means for that purpose, wherein the flowing forth of the Divine Love in the Covenant might take hold; so that a human natural substance might be in the middle, into which the divine and human imagination might enter and <margin>y immersion.</margin> y infuse itself into humanity for an <term>Ens</term> or kindler, in which the holy fire might kindle itself in the dry soul-fire.

Thus the holy fire stood opposite to the wrath and consumed the uncleanness in the human *will*, so that it might pierce through to God.

By *Moses* on *Mount Sinai*, we also see this image, where first the Father’s property in the fire manifested itself and required man’s full obedience—to live before God in holiness and draw near to him in purity through the sacrifice. If not, then he would devour them in the curse through this fire. This also was an image, type, or shadow <note>signifying</note> how human vanity should and must be purged through God’s wrath-fire. But *Israel* could not come to salvation and the love of God through the fire, and go into the Promised Land through *Moses*, but rather through *Joshua* and *Christ*.

Therefore, God brought his heavenly substance with his Covenant into water-baptism, so that there might

Covenant through the *Water-Baptism* with the outflowing Grace first into the Heavenly *Ens* <note>original: *Ens*; a philosophical term for "being" or "essence"</note> of Man—namely, into the vanished substance where the incorporated Paradisiacal Covenant of the Serpent-Destroyer <note>A reference to Christ as the one who crushes the head of the serpent (Genesis 3:15).</note> *stood*—and pierced into that same Covenant.

33. Yet seeing that the human essence, which had become earthly, should go together with such a piercing in, there must also be a *medium* for it, in which the human essence *can* take hold. For it *cannot* take hold in God’s holiness, because the human will was torn away from that; therefore, there must now be a *similitude* <note>A physical representation or likeness that corresponds to a spiritual reality.</note> in which the imagination of human nature may take hold.

34. For Christ also had this similitude—namely, the elements received from us humans—so that the Divine Imagination might take hold in the *water*. In this way, the Divine and human imagination together might awaken and kindle the incorporated Paradisiacal Covenant, in the same manner as a glimmering (*mother*) fire or tinder-fire enters the wood and begins to glow.

35. In such a manner, through baptism, a Divine "mother-fire" of the Holy Fire was introduced (a holy and Divine glimmering fire). This destroyed and broke *sin* and *death* to pieces in the inwardness of man—namely, in the vanished heavenly *essence*. 36. <note>The original text repeats the number 35 in error; it is modernized here as 36 for clarity of sequence.</note> This is now the *Water-Baptism*, for which the Holy Spirit in the inward ground is the hand. With the flowing forth of Divine Love out of Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection, the Spirit baptizes with His victory. That is, He infuses Christ’s humanity, suffering, death, and resurrection into the inward ground, and kindles the incorporated Paradisiacal Covenant with this fire, so that the dry "Rod of Aaron" <note>A biblical reference to Aaron’s rod miraculously budding (Numbers 17), symbolizing life coming from death or spiritual rebirth.</note> buds forth.

37. For with this infusion of the Holy Spirit, Christ is bestowed upon man. Man is hereby incorporated into Christ; and the heavenly *essence*, which in *Mary* assumed our human *essence*, is put on him along with the whole process of Christ. It is imprinted in his own heavenly vanished *essence* toward a New Life which *has* overcome death.

38. As a *tincture* <note>In alchemy and early chemistry, a substance that can infuse and transform the properties of another material.</note> completely tints and thoroughly penetrates metal, or as fire glows all the way through iron, so it is to be understood here concerning those who are capable of such infusion, as shall be further taught.

# Chapter 2: And what the Baptism in the New Testament is

...might be a tolerable *medium* <note>original: "Medium"; a middle ground or vehicle</note>, whereby he might introduce his love-essence into our vanished and heavenly substance, wherein the holy fire might kindle itself again.

This was done specifically because the human essence had become *earthly*. In order for man to bear or endure it, there had to be such a *medium* that the human essence could grasp. For without a *medium*, the human essence cannot take hold of God’s holiness; the human will had been torn away from it.

Therefore, God became man so that he might cause his *deity to flow into us along with his humanity*, so that we might comprehend or take hold of him. This was also so that the divine imagination <note>The creative, formative power of God's mind</note> might introduce and awaken itself in man and work together with the human imagination. Therefore, he assumed humanity so that he might work in us with the deity through the humanity.

Thus, with the water-baptism, a glimmering, mother-holy-fire was *imprinted in God’s love as a subject* or object of divine imagination and work. Through this, death—according to the *inwardness* of man—would be broken to pieces and a new life would spring forth. Just as fire begins to glimmer in wood, so was this *infusion* or baptizing an <gloss>anointing</gloss> <margin>Or *unction*.</margin> of the Holy Spirit, serving as a new oil of life in which the divine light might kindle itself.

This is now the *basis* of *water baptism*, for which the Holy Spirit in the inward ground is the *hand*. With the flowing forth of divine love out of Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection, the Spirit baptizes with his victory. That is: he infuses Christ’s humanity, suffering, death, and resurrection into man. He kindles the incorporated paradisical covenant with this fire, so that the dry rod of Aaron <note>A biblical reference to Numbers 17, symbolizing miraculous life springing from what was dead</note> begins to bud forth again.

For with this infusion of the Holy Spirit, Christ is *bestowed* upon man. Man is hereby incorporated into Christ; and the heavenly *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the fundamental being or spiritual substance</note>, which in the Virgin *Mary* assumed our human *essence*, is put on and imprinted in him for a new life, along with the entire process of Christ's work.

As a *tincture* <note>In alchemy, a substance capable of transforming or "coloring" the nature of another</note> transforms a *metal*, or as fire glows through iron, so it is to be understood here concerning those who are capable of receiving this infusion, as will be explained further.

<summary>This page explains that baptism acts as a necessary "medium" for God to reunite with earthly humanity, using the Holy Spirit to infuse Christ's life into the believer like fire through iron.</summary>

## The Third Chapter.

### A briefer and *more* fundamental instruction, how man is baptized by the *Holy Spirit*, with **Christ's suffering, death, and resurrection in body and soul.**

1. <image-desc>Ornamental drop cap 'W' featuring a foliage pattern.</image-desc>When God intended to transition his covenant from circumcision into *water baptism*, the Word of the Inspoken Grace (in which the holy fire of God resided) first *beforehand* became a man. He first assumed the woman's seed as *our* soul and humanity, so that he *might* baptize us with the Living Covenant which had become a man.

2. For the body of man, for which baptism was necessary, was made of the elements. Therefore, if it was to be baptized, the covenant first had to give itself into an elementary *medium* <note>original: "Medium"; a vehicle or middle ground</note>, as it did in the humanity of Christ. It had to sanctify that *medium* so that man might be baptized through this *medium*.

3. For it was not to be done *only* for the heavenly substance of man which vanished in Adam—in which the covenant in Paradise incorporated itself—so that it *alone* should be baptized. No; it was also for the soul and for the body made from the <term>limus</term> <note>The mud, slime, or dust of the earth</note> of the earth.

4. The *whole* man needed baptism: all three principles of all *three* worlds in man must be baptized. **Namely:** the *first* principle is the eternal nature, **namely:** the true soul's life that came out of the Inspoken Word into the body. The *second* principle is the true eternal spirit, **namely:** the holy power of the light and love (which I call in this little book the heavenly vanished <term>ens</term> <note>Essence or being</note> and substance in *Adam*) in which Adam vanished; that is, the soul's will departed from it. The *third* principle is the astral animal

### The Third Chapter.

# A Briefer and *more* fundamental Instruction, how Man is Baptized by the *Holy Ghost*, with Christ’s Suffering, Death, and Resurrection, in Body and Soul.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'W' featuring floral and vine motifs within a square border.</image-desc>

When God intended to introduce his Covenant with *Water Baptism* into humanity, the "in-spoken" Word of Grace of God’s holy fire—namely, the fiery Love—had first become Man. He first took the woman’s seed <note>A reference to the "seed of the woman" from Genesis 3:15, interpreted as the humanity of Christ.</note> upon himself—namely, *our soul*, spirit, and whole humanity—so that *he might* baptize us with the living Covenant, which was both God and Man.

For the body of man, for which baptism was necessary, was made out of the elements. Now, if it were to be baptized, the Covenant would first give itself into an elementary *medium* <note>original: "Medium"; here meaning a physical means or channel.</note>, as it did in the humanity of Christ, and sanctify that *medium*, so that man might be baptized through it.

For this was not to be done *only* for the heavenly substance which vanished in *Paradise*. In that substance—the most inward ground of humanity—the Covenant in Paradise incorporated itself. It was not that this ground *alone* should be baptized; no, it was also for the soul, and for the body made from the *slime* <note>original: "Limus"; the dust or clay of the earth from which Adam was formed.</note> of the earth.

The *whole* man needs baptism. All the Three Principles—that is, the properties of all *three worlds* in man—must be baptized. 

1. *The first Principle* is the Eternal Nature, the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"; in Boehme's theosophy, the chaos or potentiality from which all things arise.</note>, out of which the visible world has sprung forth; it is the ground of the true Eternal Soul which came into the body through God’s breathing into it. 
2. *The second Principle* is the true Eternal Spirit, namely, the Holy Light’s Power. (In this little book, I call this power the heavenly *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; the spiritual being or substance.</note> or substance that vanished in *Adam*). This vanished in *Adam* at the Fall, as the soul’s will departed from it into earthliness,

Now when the Father’s fire is stirred and kindled, then the Divine Light of the great *sweet love* shines in the opened name *JESUS* out of the Holy Name *JEHOVAH* in the center of the Father’s fire, out of the unity of God, as the *beams of divine grace* in the soul; for the soul is unlocked with this *glimpse*, and then suddenly the *light of love* takes in the overlooked ground and fills it with the *substance of love*.
And thus the *Son*, namely: the *love of the Father*, receives the soul, and sanctifies it, and thoroughly penetrates it, as fire thoroughly penetrates iron. And that is what Christ says: *Father, the men were yours, and you have given them to me; and I give them eternal life. And thus the soul lives in the *Father*, and yet is governed and sanctified by the *Son’s office*, and its anxious *fire-life* is merely a *burning of love*. But the *Father’s concussion* or *shrill sounding* is necessary so that the soul’s fire-source may be touched, so that the soul may find what it is; for it is thereby brought into continual *repentance and humility* when the *beam of God's wrath often touches it*, so that it considers its fall and is at no time secure (for it has a great enemy near it in its flesh and blood, namely *Satan’s poison*), so that it may no longer run on in its own will as it did formerly. 8. Thus now the Divine Light of the Great Love is present in the opened name JESUS, which comes out of JEHOVAH in the center of the Father’s fire. When the Father’s fire unlocks the fiery soul, then the light of the Divine Love can shine into it and introduce the Love *Ens* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term meaning "essence" or "being"</note> into it. For as soon as the wrath’s fire unlocks the soul, it *instantly* receives the love-fire of the Son and enters into it, just as fire glows through iron, or as a *tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy, a substance that can transform or permeate another</note> penetrates metal.

9. And thus is the soul *given* from the Father to the Son, and thus the Son *gives* it the love-fire—namely, the eternal life. For if the Father’s property did not move within the shut-up soul, then the soul would remain in its compression in eternal death, and the light *could not* be manifested in it.

<margin>Commotion or contrition.</margin>
10. The shrill sounding and kindling of the Father’s fire is the fountain of human repentance, where man trembles before sin. For in that fire-glance, the false, compressed vanity of *sins* in the darkness of the soul is made manifest; and the shining-in of the Son’s love is the meek love-oil that unites and tempers the *contrition* or shrill sounding again.

# How Man is Baptized: Chapter 3

12. ...considered: By the *Water*—namely, by the element of the Body of Christ—the true Adamic man <note>original: "Adamicall Man"; referring to the human nature created in Adam</note> is baptized. This man was created in Adam regarding the body, formed out of the substance of the outward world. Here the Holy Spirit baptizes, who proceeds from the Father and the Son. His proceeding is the formation of the world; the world is the "outspoken," formed Word, and it is *the Spirit of God* that has formed it.

13. For He is to be understood as existing in all *three worlds*, acting in each world according to its specific property. In the Father’s wrath, according to the *Darkness*, He is the flame of *painfulness*. In the Eternal Light, He is the *Love-flame of God*. And in this world, in the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note>, He is the *shaper* and *master-craftsman* of every thing, acting in every thing according to its nature. As the "Separator" <note>The internal power that divides and distinguishes qualities in a substance</note> of a thing is, so also is the outflowing Spirit from the "outspoken" Word within that thing.

14. Regarding the substance of the outward world, it should not be understood that the spirit of its substance *is* called *God* itself; rather, it is the outflowing Spirit within the "outspoken" Word of God, which has its foundation in the Word of God.

15. The outflowing Spirit of the outward creaturely and natural life has flowed forth out of God’s Love and Wrath, out of *Light* and *Darkness*—namely, out of the first and second Principles <note>The "Principles" are Boehme's terms for the dark/fire world and the light/love world</note>—as well as out of Eternal Nature and the *Speaking* of the Word. It stands with its foundation in God’s Speaking, for the Eternal Word <margin>Or, breathes forth that outspoken Spirit.</margin> <gloss>breathes</gloss> itself out with that Spirit into a creaturely life.

16. That same exhaled Spirit is the *outward* creaturely life in every creature, according to its specific nature. It is the Soul of the *Outward* World—the beginning soul, a life made of the four elements.

Its power is a fiery and light-filled *Constellation* <note>The "Constellation" refers to the internal astral or governing influence on a person's nature</note>. Whatever the entire outward Constellation is in its own power, this Spirit is the same within itself. Yet, it exists as a "shut up" Constellation that lies in the *Temperament* <note>The balanced state of the elements</note>, and yet it unfolds itself in every life and makes itself distinct according to that life’s property.

<margin>Text: By.</margin> <gloss>By</gloss> In temporary creatures, it acts with a temporary property; <gloss>and</gloss> in eternal creatures, it acts with an eternal property.

In Man, it acts with a temporary and fragile, corruptible property, and also with an eternal one.

This *eternal* part is attached to the Eternal Exhaled Word (to the Eternal Exhaling). Because of this, Man shall arise and come again on the *Last Day* in his visible image, leaving behind corruptibility. He shall be presented before God’s <margin>Or, fiery trial.</margin> Judgment and be put to the great <gloss>fiery trial</gloss> <note>original: "fires-Proba"; a test by fire</note>, where the mortal part shall separate itself from the eternal.

<summary>This page explains the role of the Holy Spirit as the "master-craftsman" of the physical and spiritual worlds, describing how the "outspoken Word" of God gives life to all creatures and prepares man for the final judgment.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Spirit, Three Worlds, Spiritus Mundi, Adamic Man, Outspoken Word, Constellation, Last Day, Fire-Proba, Baptism</keywords>

--- Page 85 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the mystical nature of Baptism and the role of the Holy Spirit in the creation and sustenance of the world.</meta>

By the *Water*—namely, by the element of the body of Christ, the true Adamic man <note>original: "Adamicall Man"; referring to the perfected human state before the Fall</note> *out of the elements* which was created in Adam—understand that the substance of the outward world is baptized. For here the Holy Spirit baptizes, who proceeds from the Father and the Son. His proceeding is the formation of the world, and the world is the "outspoken Word"; it is the Spirit of God that has formed that *outspoken Word*.

He is to be understood as existing in all *Three Principles* or worlds, acting in each world according to its specific property. **First**, in the Father’s wrath according to the Darkness, He is the flame of *painfulness*. **Secondly**, in the Eternal Light, He is the *love-flame* of God. And **Thirdly**, in this world, in the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Spiritu Mundi"</note>, He is the *former* and master-worker of everything. He works in everything according to its own property; as the separator or *formative power* <note>original: "Archæus"; a Paracelsian term for the vital force that directs growth and healing</note> of a thing is, from which it arises, so also is the outflowing Spirit from the outspoken Word present in everything.

And it is *not* to be understood that the spirit of the outward substance is itself called *God*; rather, it is the outflowing Spirit in the outspoken Word of God, which stands with its foundation in the Word of God.

The *outflowing Spirit* of the outward natural and creaturely life has flowed forth out of God’s love and wrath, out of *Light* and *Darkness*—namely, out of the first and second Principles, out of Eternal Nature, and out of the *speaking* of the Word. For the Eternal Word has breathed itself with that Spirit into a creaturely, image-like life.

It is the soul of the *outward world*, a life of the four elements. Its power is like a fiery and light-filled *constellation*. Whatever the whole outward constellation is in itself in its power, that same Spirit is everywhere alone in itself; yet it exists as a hidden constellation that lies in the *balanced temperament* <note>original: "Temperament"; the harmonious balance of the four qualities or elements</note>, and yet it unfolds itself in every life and makes itself distinct according to the property of every life.

### Note.

<margin>In the year 1624.</margin>
*Courteous Reader, this is what our author—blessed in the spirit of high knowledge—readily began to bring to an easier understanding for the simple-minded, but he has not finished it. For in the year of our Lord 1624 <note>original: "A. O. 1624" (Anno Orbis/Otii)</note>, in his Jubilee year, or the 50th year of his age, he went, according to the Mystery, back into his grave, or his magic and mental principle.* <note>This note explains that the author, Jakob Böhme, died before completing this specific explanation of his complex theology.</note>

### How Man is Baptized Ch. 3.

17. Understand us now here rightly in this way, concerning the Baptism of this *Outward Spirit*: The Inward *Holy Spirit* of *Divine Love* kindles the *Outflowing Spirit* and anoints it with *Divine Power*: For the humanity of *Christ* according to our *Outward Spirit* was anointed by the Holy Ghost. And that same outward Spirit of Christ, which in Christ’s person in the elements of the body ruled after a *human manner*, in the Covenant of his Testament in the Water-Baptism, anoints the inward ground of the *Water*; and the anointed power in the water, in the word of the Covenant, anoints the true *Man*, who in *Adam* was created out of the *Limus* of the Earth; and so also it anoints the *Spiritus Mundi*, the Spirit of the world; namely, the true *Astral Soul*, which at the Last Day shall come again and be *tried*.

<margin>Note.</margin>
18. Now understand us dearly and well, for it benefits us much: This anointing of Water-Baptism is anointed with Christ's suffering, death, and resurrection; it is anointed with Christ’s shedding of his blood, with which heavenly blood he changed God’s wrath into love and *besprinkled* Death in the human property, with which he anointed the Earth when he *shed forth this, his blood* upon and into it. Note also: It was anointed with it when Christ yielded his anointed life of the outward humanity received from us, in his death, into this *Spiritus Mundi*, this spirit of the world again, and anointed that also with his anointing in his death: namely, where his outward mortal soul, received from us (which died on the Cross and yielded itself into the *hand of God* as into his Outspoken Word) went out of the body into the elements. Also: It is anointed with it where this, his outward soul, with the Eternal Soul in which the Holy Ghost was, penetrated through Death and Hell. And also with it where the Holy Ghost in the Word received this, his dead Astral Soul, out of death and brought it through death with his resurrection into Eternal Life, where God's love anointed it with Eternal Life.

19. This anointing of Christ is understood in the Water-Baptism as in the Covenant of God, for God anoints in the humanity of Christ the elements of the body, together with the *mortal* and *immortal* soul, and brings Christ with this anointing into his death, and into the wrath, and through death into Eternal Life. And this anointing (in which Christ has overcome God’s wrath, sin, death, the devil, and hell) God has ordained with his Covenant of the Old Testament in the Water-Baptism, and offers it now to every man.

20. And so now if a man desires it, and inclines himself to it as a man and not a beast, then the Holy Ghost *baptizes* the inward ground, namely, his temple, which he himself possesses, namely, the true E-

Now if I sin, it is not I, but sin in the Evil flesh that does it: Also: so then I now serve God with the mind of the anointed Ground, and with the Mind of the false flesh I serve the Law of sin.*

Our Conversation is in Heaven: Also Rom. 6:15. Gal. 2:17. Shall we then that belong to Christ still be sinners? far be it from us:* where he means the Anointed Ground, and *not* the bestial Monster of Earthliness, full of Evil inclinations, which stands in the wrath of God, the Condemnation; and shall not inherit the Kingdom of God, which yet in this [life] time, clings to the right anointed Man.

25. The anointed ground <note>original: "Ground"; referring to the spiritual foundation or inner soul</note> is the Christian, and not the outward, coarse beast <note>the physical, animalistic body</note>. It is the *anointing* which daily breaks the head of the monster, the serpent—namely, the evil earthly will. This is where the struggle in man exists, where one property wills evil and the other wills good, and whichever overcomes drives the man to act. *Therefore, man's works shall and must be judged, and every one shall be gathered into its proper barn: namely, that which is done in the power of the anointing shall be gathered into the Kingdom of God; and that which is done in the power of the monster, the serpent, shall be gathered into damnation, into the darkness of wrath and death.*

---

### *The Fourth Chapter.*

## Concerning outward water baptism performed by human hands: who is worthy to perform such baptism? And what baptized infant worthily receives this baptism? How it fares with the unworthy hand, as well as with the unworthy baptized infant.

->*In these times, this is highly necessary to be considered.*<-

1. When God established the testament of his holy covenant of grace in Paradise, moving beyond the types and sacrifices, and intended to transform circumcision into water baptism, he then brought forth a *branch* out of his covenant in a human manifestation and began the water baptism through him. This is clearly seen in *John the Baptist*, who was proclaimed by an angelic message or embassy and was, moreover, from the lineage of the high priests.

2. His incarnation, or becoming man, did not come from the will of the flesh; for his father *Zachary* and mother *Elizabeth* were elderly people, advanced in years, whose physical capacity for reproduction <note>original: "fleshly sperme or seede"</note> was clearly dead, and the natural ability of man was entirely gone. The seed of *Zachary* and *Elizabeth* was raised up through the moving of the Holy Ghost out of the paradisiacal covenant of grace, within their property of reproduction <note>original: "Spermatick Property"</note> which had by then completely vanished.

<summary>This page explains that a Christian's works are judged based on whether they stem from spiritual anointing or earthly will, and introduces the divine origins of John the Baptist as the transition from circumcision to water baptism.</summary>
<keywords>anointing, spiritual warfare, John the Baptist, Covenant of Grace, Zachary and Elizabeth, water baptism, incarnation</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the spiritual lineage and office of John the Baptist in relation to the incarnation of Christ.</meta>

3. Indeed, he <note>John the Baptist</note> came out of the human ground, but not out of human *ability*. For the anointing in the Covenant awakened it, so that their sperm <note>original: "sperme"; here meaning the biological seed or essence of life</note> was unlocked above the course and power of Nature’s own ability. This *Holy Spirit* raised up a seed out of the human essence, and for that purpose chose <margin>a The Seed.</margin> it in its very beginning.

4. But the anointing of *John* was performed during *Mary’s* greeting, when she came to old *Elizabeth* and the divine anointing stirred in her (understand this to be in *Mary*) arising from *Christ’s* Incarnation, or his becoming Man. When *Mary*, full of this anointing, greeted *Elizabeth*, then the Incarnation of *Christ*—diffused with the anointing through *Mary’s* greeting—entered into *John* in his mother’s womb. This happened not only in *John*, but also in his mother *Elizabeth*, so that she was full of this anointing; and both mothers prophesied in the Spirit of this anointing.

5. And this is the Baptism of *John*: when he was anointed with the Incarnation of *Christ*, by *Christ* himself, and received the Baptism of the Holy Spirit from the Covenant while in his mother’s womb. This was so that he should be a forerunner and proclaimer, declaring that *the time of the Anointing and the fulfilling of God’s Covenant was at hand*. It signaled that God has introduced his Covenant of the anointing of *Grace* into humanity, and that the Covenant has become a Man. From this point forward, God will pour out his anointing through the humanity of *Christ* by an elementary *medium* or means. This no longer consists of *fire*, as in the Old Testament, but in the love and meekness of *water*, showing that the time of Grace is at hand, wherein God has placed his anointing into humanity.

6. Furthermore, we see that God began no new Covenant there, but only a new *ordinance*. For *John* allowed himself to be circumcised, as did *Christ*. *John* took the power and calling out of the Covenant of circumcision and out of the sacrifices of fire, and by God’s command brought them into the *Water-Baptism*. This was to signify that from then on, sin should be drowned by the death of *Christ* in his love and meekness, and *no longer burn in the fire*, as was done by *Israel* in the symbolic types <note>original: "Type"; a theological term for a person or event in the Old Testament that foreshadows something in the New Testament</note>.

7. Moreover, we see by *John* that he did not yet baptize for the forgiveness of sins, but for repentance—namely, with the Spirit of the anointing toward repentance. This sounds through the hearts of men and *prepares the way for the Spirit of Christ*, which unlocks the doors of men’s hearts and souls. This was so that after <margin>b Namely, John the Baptist.</margin> him, the *King of Glory might enter in at those opened gates*. Concerning this, *David* says: *Lift up your heads, O you gates; and be lifted up, you everlasting doors; and the King of Glory shall come in* (Psalm 24:7, 9). And as *John* witnesses: *He baptized with water unto repentance and forgiveness of sins, yet he is not Christ; but after him comes he who has the winnowing fan in his hand, who will baptize with the fire of the Spirit* (Luke 3:16, 17).

<summary>This page explains that John the Baptist received his divine anointing in the womb through Mary's visitation to Elizabeth, marking the transition from the Old Testament's "fire" sacrifices to the New Testament's "water" baptism of grace. It emphasizes John's role as the forerunner who prepares human hearts for the indwelling of Christ.</summary>

# Of the Outward Water-Baptism Ch. 4.

8. Also we see in *John*, that he had to be the son of a priest, those who were conversant with circumcision and sacrifices. He had to come out of the Law of the Covenant and allow himself to be circumcised and put on the Covenant, so that he might, with the Spirit of the Covenant and of circumcision, *begin* the Water Baptism; namely, the Ordinance of Grace and forgiveness. For sin should be destroyed now no more by fire, but by Love, which God manifested in the Covenant through *Christ*; it <insert>sin</insert> should be drowned and changed by God's meekness.

<margin>c The absolution from sins.</margin>
9. Therefore also, God ordained this means of Water Baptism for this destroying of sins: to signify that sin should be drowned in the blood of Christ, in his Love and *Grace*, and that man should *henceforth* have an open gate of Grace to God in love and meekness, so that now with the repentant sorrow for sins, and with the $^c$ forsaking of sins, he may enter through this gate into Grace.

10. And we see here in *John*, the beginner of the Water Baptism, what man is worthy to baptize with this new Grace Covenant; namely, such a person as has also beforehand been *baptized* with this Grace, as *John* was by Christ, who was baptized even in his mother’s womb. For flesh and blood *without* this anointing cannot baptize; for this
<margin>d Or, Power.</margin>
might stands not in the $^d$ authority of man, but in Christ's $^d$ authority.

<margin>Note.</margin>
11. A *Christian*, in whom is the anointing of Christ, baptizes with the anointing of Christ, for the Holy Trinity baptizes with the Grace Covenant of the anointing—with Christ's Incarnation or becoming man, with his suffering, death, and victory. Now if any will baptize, they must not only baptize with the hand and water baptism, but also with the *faith* of the anointing.

12. An unfaithful or unbelieving baptizer does no more in this high work of baptism than the font-stone <note>*or basin*</note> does, which holds the water. For though he sprinkles or pours water and uses the words of *Christ*, yet he effects nothing thereby, but is dumb <note>*or dead*</note> in the working, unless the Covenant of Christ works and baptizes. He is but merely a *medium* or means,
<margin>e Or, Thing.</margin>
like a workless <note>inanimate</note> $^e$ substance that does not itself work together in this Covenant, but only does the work in which God works in respect of His Covenant.

13. This is not to be understood as if the work by the unworthy
<margin>f Or, Ineffectual</margin>
hand were therefore wholly $^f$ powerless; no, the Covenant of God
<margin>g Absents or withdraws itself.</margin>
$^g$ does not sever itself for the sake of the unworthy hand, which is but a *medium* or means. For faithful parents, who stand in the anointing of this Covenant and have put on the Covenant, they also have the anointing in their seed and generate children out of the property

# Ch. 4. By Man's Hand.

property of their bodies and souls: now if they be baptized in body and soul, and have put on the anointing of Christ, why then not also the <term>Ens</term> of their bodies? If they be the Temples of the Holy Ghost (who dwells in them) and eat Christ's flesh and drink his blood, so that Christ is in them and they in Christ, as Christ says in *John 6:56*, why then not also the fruit of their bodies? For Christ says indeed (*Matthew 7:18*), *a good tree cannot bring forth bad fruit, and a bad tree cannot bring forth good fruit.* If the eye of the spirit be light, then the whole body is light; if the leaven be holy, then the whole lump is holy, *Matthew 6, Romans 11.*

14. John was baptized in the mother's womb through the Spirit of Christ out of Mary's voice, as was also his mother Elizabeth. And we see clearly how it came to pass; for as Elizabeth heard the Spirit of Mary, the Holy Ghost moved in her, and also in her fruit alike; they received the Baptism of Christ out of his humanity alike, the mother with the son. Why then not also now at present, in holy parents in whom the anointing is? *For such as the tree is, such also is its fruit.* But the Covenant in the Baptism is therefore intended so that everyone should <unclear>bind</unclear> themselves with their own will, as a particular branch on the tree, and as a life of their own, put on the Covenant of Christ, as through the outward *medium* or *means* ordained for that purpose.

<margin>h Godparents. i Transfer or deliver over.</margin>
15. This is not to be understood as meaning that a child of honest, anointed parents, who has attained life, is not in the anointing of Christ even if it dies before the Baptism. It has indeed put on the anointing of its parents, for it has existed out of their substance, out of their baptized souls’ and bodies’ substance. It only needs, if it lives, to enter into the image of the visible Covenant as a life of its own, and with its will give itself up into that which it inherits from its parents. But seeing a child does not understand this, therefore the parents do it with their faith, and everyone of those who are called to that work—namely, the $^h$ witnesses or godfathers and godmothers—who in their faith's desire, with their prayer, $^i$ recommend the child to the Covenant of Christ. They present it in the Covenant of Christ to the Holy Trinity and desire the Covenant to be upon this child.

16. All these, both the parents and bystanders—namely, the faithful baptizer and witnesses—work with their faith in the property of the child, and reach out to it with their faith the Covenant of Christ. For the will of their faith takes the child's will (the child being without understanding) into their faith's desire, and so they bear the child's will in their own will, with their anointing, into the Covenant of Christ—namely, before the Holy Trinity.

# Of Outward Water-Baptism Chapter 4

In the words of Christ, who says: *Matthew 28:19. Go hence into all the world, baptizing all nations, in the name of the Father, Son, and Holy Ghost:* Upon Christ’s command they baptize men. The baptizer baptizes with the hand and with faith; and the parents, together with the witnesses <note>godparents</note>, baptize with their faith’s desire, with the anointed will. They infuse it into Christ’s Covenant, and Christ infuses himself therein with his blood, death, and victory—namely, with the victory of his death—and kindles the faith-essence <note>original: "faiths-Ens"; the spiritual substance or being of faith</note> of the child with his love-fire.

18. Thus the grain of mustard seed of the love-fire sows itself into the essence of the child’s soul and body as a glimmering <margin>k Hatcher, breeder, or ember.</margin> mother <note>original: "Mother"; here used in the sense of a source, womb, or nurturing origin</note> of divine love. This mother-source afterwards, when the child gains understanding of its *own*, is further kindled through faith, repentance, and prayer, and becomes a high-shining light. And when afterwards the mouth of faith eats Christ’s *flesh* and drinks *his blood*, then this holy mother love-fire attains holy oil for the burning of its life.

19. With baptism is implanted the humanity of Christ according to the holy love-water—namely, the *water of eternal life*, in which water the holy fire may burn. And with the Supper <note>Holy Communion</note> is shared the holy tincture <note>a spiritual essence or medicinal virtue</note> in the blood and flesh of Christ—namely, the true burning love-fire, a spiritual anointing oil out of the divine fire and light, which the water of love (the Baptism-Covenant) kindles. And though a child be born of *holy parents*, and in the mother’s womb be clearly in this Covenant, yet it ought also to put on this Covenant in its own self-subsisting person, in its own life’s <margin>Note.</margin> will. This is because in the flesh, sin and uncleanness are propagated and inherited together; therefore, the child should put on this Covenant for itself. For the child ought and must, with the Covenant of Grace, break the head of the serpent in the flesh, and therefore it ought also to put on Christ with his victory. For Christ now offers himself to the child; it ought therefore in its own person to receive him with its own will, and give its own will up to him.

20. For baptism is nothing else but a marriage or contract with God <margin>l Text: upon.</margin> in Christ’s blood and death, wherein Christ binds himself to man with his victory and resurrection, and places man therein. And man in baptism gives up his Adam-like <note>original: "Adamicall"; referring to the fallen nature of Adam</note> contrary will to the *death of Christ*, and desires to die to his own will in the death of Christ, and to rise again through Christ’s resurrection out of Christ’s death—with and in Christ—to a new will, and to live and to will with Christ. For we are, through baptism with Christ’s victory, planted anew in the Word of God. For man is the outspoken, imaged Word of God, which has brought his will in *Adam* from the speaking of God into a private will and speaking, and has become treacherous...

<summary>This page explains that baptism is a spiritual marriage and covenant where Christ's "love-fire" is planted as a seed in the child's soul. It emphasizes that even children of believers must personally enter this covenant to overcome inherited sin and align their will with God's Word.</summary>

<keywords>Baptism, Covenant of Grace, Love-fire, Ens, Tincture, Adamic will, New Birth, Christ's Victory</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the spiritual mechanics of Baptism, specifically focusing on the roles of the parents, witnesses, and the minister in the initiation of a child into the Covenant.</meta>

# Chapter 4. By Man's Hand.

...treacherous to the *Word* of God: and with the Baptism, the imaged word <note>original: "Imaged Word"; referring to the human soul as an image of God's word</note> promises and incorporates itself again with the Eternal Speaking of God, that it will speak and will according to God: namely, *God's righteousness and truth.*

<margin>In or into.</margin>
21. Baptism is an *oath* which man swears into God, when he renounces the Devil and his kingdom, and gives himself up to God as his own property, namely, to be a *Temple of God*. And though a child does not understand *this*, yet the baptizer, as well as the parents and witnesses, should understand it and introduce their faith into the child's will. Thus, with their introduced *will* joined to the child's will, they together plunge or sink themselves down with this oath into God's Covenant through the *Word* of Christ, by his command, in the water-baptism. They should take hold of the command of Christ within themselves—that is, in their faith—and so, through their *understanding*, enter into the Covenant along with the child's <margin>Or, ignorance.</margin> lack of understanding. In the place of, and with the will of the child, they make such a promise to God in their own will. The *parents* especially may and ought to do this, seeing the child has sprung out of their life and substance, as a branch grows out <margin>Thus,</margin> of a tree trunk; so they *also have power* to include the *will* of the child within the will of their faith, and with the child's will to give it up and bind or unite it unto God.

22. Moreover, the *baptizer* has this in his power, who by the command of Christ stands there in Christ's place. He brings the command of Christ in his mouth and baptizes *with his hand* upon the command of Christ. Such a person should be the *anointed* of Christ and enter into that work *through the door of Christ* <note>original: "through the Doore of Christ"; meaning entering the ministry through true spiritual calling and faith</note>, or he is merely like a piece of wood or a clod of earth standing by as an *earthly medium*. In such a case, he himself does *not* baptize along with his own faith, but is only an outward instrument of the Covenant, like the axe with which the carpenter hews. Though indeed, he is not entirely like the axe that cuts, but like the *hand* that holds the axe: he neither cuts nor works together in the work of the *Spirit*, but is only the outward instrument and performs only an outward act. He does not reach the Covenant itself, but only the water, and brings a dead word in his life; yet the office of Christ remains in the command.

23. The *living Word* in the Covenant which baptizes there does not have its origin in the power of a wicked mouth, but out of the *command* in the Covenant. The wicked mouth brings the outward *literal word* as a minister of it, but the Covenant brings the *Living Word in power*. If the parents and witnesses are faithful, they reach the child forth with their faith to the Covenant, and the wicked baptizer with his mouth and hand is but an instrument to it—in the same way that a clock strikes and sounds and yet has no life, but gives notice and understanding to the *living*...

<summary>This page explains that Baptism is a sacred oath where parents and witnesses exercise their faith on behalf of an infant, binding the child's will to God's Covenant. It also clarifies that the spiritual validity of the sacrament depends on Christ's command rather than the personal righteousness of the minister performing the rite.</summary>

24. Thus also the holy name baptizes through the wicked baptizer, and not the evil mouth; but he must be an instrument that pronounces the holy name, which works not from his pronouncing, but from the Name and Covenant, through the introduction of faith by the faithful parents and witnesses. For the Covenant is itself a Faith [or Believing], namely: God's Word and Mouth. The Covenant baptizes them all that give themselves up into it, whether worthily or unworthily, whether the faith of the baptizer and of the parents and witnesses be there or not—but with great difference, as the Scripture says: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse, Psalm 18:26.* Also, *as the people are, such a God they have*: and as Saint Paul says of the Supper: *Therefore they received it unto judgment, because they distinguish not the Lord's body.*

25. For where God's love is present, there his wrath is also present: where light is, there is also fire. The Ens <note>A technical term referring to the spiritual essence or "being" of a thing.</note> of faith presses only through the fire forth into light. The Covenant stands firm; the child is baptized with the Covenant; the Covenant receives it, although there were only a wicked baptizer, and also such witnesses without faith. The wicked man that draws near to his Covenant is to God in his Covenant a good savor to the condemnation of death, and the holy is a good savor to life, 2 Corinthians 2:15, 16.*

26. What else can be, if there are evil parents without faith, but that they also beget wicked children? For as the stock is, so also is the fruit. And now if they do not convert and, through true earnest repentance and prayer, incorporate their fruit into Christ, and send them with faith to this Covenant, but instead invite such wicked witnesses or gossips <note>Godparents or baptismal sponsors.</note> without faith to it, and there is also a wicked baptizer, who shall baptize here? Shall the love of God in the Covenant of Grace baptize? Might it not be as Saint Paul says of the Supper: *That the wicked receive it unto judgment?* Now if a man makes a mock of the Covenant, how will God's glory appear among the scorners?

<margin>Or, False.</margin>
Scorners? There it is rightly said: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse.* If the seed is bad, even if a man casts it into good ground, bad fruit still grows from it; for it draws from the good ground an Ens <note>A technical term referring to the spiritual essence or "being" of a thing.</note> of its own likeness to itself.

27. How then can it go well there, where a child springs from parents who are quite wicked, and where only wicked witnesses are called to it—who stand there only for status and pride, or for human favor, and are called for such reasons—when there is no faith or good will, much less an earnest prayer? Instead, only an *earthly Lucifer* stands before the Covenant of Christ, and the baptizer is also a dead person. Who awakens the Holy Covenant here? Just as the faith is that moves or stirs the Covenant, such is also the manifestation of the Covenant, and so is also the baptism.

28. Christ says, *Suffer little children to come unto me, for of such is the kingdom of God* (Matthew 10:14; Chapter 19:13); but he means children, not wolves and beasts. He bids them to come to him, and not to bring them to him in the devil's pride and pomp, with stately, high-minded, false, and unfaithful people who only despise the humility of Christ and scorn love. It must be in earnest if a man will enter through the wrath of God into love.

29. Reason says this: What does it matter to the child if it is begotten of wicked parents and is brought by evil people to the Covenant of Christ?

Answer: Yes indeed, what does it matter to God—*who does not will the death of a sinner*—that wicked parents beget wicked children, and that a man only scorns his Covenant of Grace? Shall he therefore cast pearls before swine? The parents indeed know very well that God says *he will punish the sin of the parents on the children to the third and fourth generation* (Deuteronomy 5:9). Shall he then pour his love into their wicked wills, when they do not actually desire that love, nor is there any earnestness there, but merely the performance of a custom? They do it only with a hypocritical show without sincerity, and they will make use of such people for it who only scorn the simplicity of Christ with their pride.

30. To be conversant with the Testaments <note>A term often used by Böhme to refer to the Sacraments of Baptism and the Lord's Supper.</note> and Covenant of Christ, and to make use of them, will require great earnestness—not only in *appearance* but in *power*. For the Covenant of God in Christ is made through his blood and death for the purpose that whoever will partake of this Covenant and these Testaments should convert with *their will* and enter into repentance, and die to the inherited false lust in the Covenant through Christ’s death, and be born anew out of the Covenant of these Testaments.

31. For baptism consists not alone in water, but in the Word

# Ch. 4. by Man's Hand.

in the child's will, for the child has *not yet* a willing, neither to Good nor Evil, but their Faith encompasses itself in the child's Life, namely: in Soul and Spirit: which may well be, as all souls have their origin from One, and so they are in the Center, one only Ground, namely: in the Word of God, out of which the Soul has taken its origin, and wherein they all stand together in the Ground, for this encompassing in [ the child ] is nothing else, but to introduce their *love-will* into the child: and although a Man cannot do that in his *own power* and Might, yet if the will of Man encompasses itself in *God's Word* and Promise, and takes hold of the *Promised Grace*, together with the command that we should do this thing, then is the *Possibility* there: For the Promised Word gives and works the ability in Man's Will, and gives the Deed: God's will takes Man's will resigned up to him, and *does it through his Might with Man's will*.

35. Therefore say I, it is a dangerous thing, to baptize children without the Faith of the Parents, and of those who Baptize, and the rest who are present for the work: The Scripture says, *Heb. 11: 6. Without Faith it is impossible to please God:* But this Faith is not a History or Knowledge, that a Man only gives assent to it, and believes that it is God's work: No, it is an earnest, desiring co-working, a pressing into the Promised Grace, an earnest consideration of our inherited sin, and of the great earnestness of God, and how he through this Grace-Covenant receives us again as children, and pours his Grace into us *with this work*.

<margin>Turning toward God.</margin>
<margin>Or, Advancement.</margin>
36. Those who are present should with great humility and inversion <note>A turning inward or spiritual concentration</note> toward God, with earnest prayer, apply themselves to this Covenant, and well consider what they purpose to do there, for they stand even before the Covenant of the Holy Trinity, before God's open face, and have to deal with God and Man, and should well imprint in themselves the bitter sufferings and dying of Jesus Christ: into which a child is baptized, of which they are co-working witnesses according to the faith: and not stand before this Covenant like a harlot before a mirror <note>Modernized from "Whore before a Glasse"</note>, with proud hearts, as if it were a worldly honor which a man should make use of for the occasion: such people who have no understanding and faith toward it, and only come to it with proud hearts, are not helpful <note>Modernized from "profitable"</note> to this Covenant, but a hindrance; they hinder others, who gaze upon the pride and showiness <note>Modernized from "bravery"</note>, and thereby forget the earnestness.

37. The Holy Spirit baptizes the soul and the *spirit* from Christ’s blood and death with his victory; and the holy element of the spiritual *water* baptizes the *body* of the four elements for the *resurrection of the dead*, and baptizes the *serpent’s being* <note>Ens</note> and the awakened, introduced *poison* to die <note>or for the dead</note>.

Note.</margin>
39. It is a substantial working infusion of the covenant of God. Therefore, if the infant baptized is to worthily receive it, there must be earnestness. A *Christian*, who is a *Christian* in *Christ*, in whom *Christ* works, lives, and *dwells*, he should baptize; for he must enter into this *sheepfold* by the *door of Christ*—namely, through Christ’s Spirit—and not climb up elsewhere; otherwise, he is a *thief* and a *murderer*, and comes only so that he may rob and steal the honor of *Christ* and deceive men. He should be *a true shepherd and not a hireling*. Here, no babbling or making a show is of any use, but there must be earnestness, for it is an earnest matter with God. All jangling and disputation about this work is an unprofitable thing, and furthermore hurtful. Men should take hold of it with earnestness and follow the *command of Christ*, and believe what *Christ* has said.

40. No more belongs to this work but *faith* and *water*, and *earnest prayer* in true *repentance*, with such a *will* that one man would help to release another from *death*, the *devil*, and *hell*, and help to introduce him with himself into the *Kingdom of God*. That is the whole process that belongs to this. <margin>Note.</margin> *Everyone who is a true Christian in Christ is worthy to be a bystander and present at this work; but he who is not such a one is unworthy, whether he be the baptizer or a bystander—one as much as the other. There is no respect of persons with God. In Christ we are ONE; He is the stock, and we are the branches. He works his work through his fellow-members, as the stock of the tree generates its fruit through its twigs and branches. The stock uses no strange tree for its twigs; so also Christ only uses his members for his work of his fruit.*

---

### Concerning
# The Holy Baptism.

<meta>This page serves as a brief cautionary preface or "Intimation" to the reader, following the biblical epigraph on the previous page. It emphasizes the spiritual nature of the work and warns against purely intellectual interpretation.</meta>

# An Intimation or Instruction
## to the R E A D E R.

Though indeed I here dive and search *sublimely and deeply, and set it down very clearly, yet the reader must be told this much: that without the Spirit of God, it will be a mystery to him and not understood.*

Therefore let *everyone look well to what he judges, so that he does not fall into the judgment of God and be captivated by his own* <term>Turba</term>*, and so that his own reason does not distract him; this I say, meaning well, and give it to the reader to ponder* <note>in his mind</note>.

-> JACOB BEHM of old Seidenberg. <-

<summary>In this brief preface, Jacob Boehme warns the reader that his profound spiritual writings can only be understood through the Spirit of God rather than human reason. He cautions against passing judgment on the text to avoid spiritual confusion or divine judgment.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual insight, Turba, divine mystery, human reason, Seidenberg, discernment</keywords>

--- Page 102 ---
<meta>This page serves as the Table of Contents for the second book, focusing on the theology and practice of the Lord's Supper.</meta>

# The Contents of the Chapters in this Second little Book, Concerning the Holy Supper of Jesus Christ.

1. Concerning the foundation of the Old Testament: how this Testament was clearly represented as a *Type* <note>original: "Type"; a symbolic foreshadowing or prophetic model</note> among the *Jews*.

2. Concerning the Supper of the New Testament: how the Type has transitioned into *Essence* [or Substance].

3. How the *Disciples* ate and drank the flesh and blood of Christ.

4. Concerning the *difference* in the participation of it: what the wicked receive with this Testament, and how a person should prepare themselves so that they may be truly worthy.

5. Concerning the contention and *strife* in the high schools or universities about Christ’s Testaments: how that strife, which has filled the face of the Earth, is a vain and very harmful thing. It arises in the anger of God from the Devil’s desire and will, whereby all brotherly love and unity is destroyed, the highly precious Name of GOD is blasphemed, and the Holy GHOST—in his divine wonders—is judged and despised by *Reason* <note>original: "Reason"; here referring to human intellect operating without spiritual illumination</note>.

<summary>This table of contents outlines five chapters regarding the Holy Supper, moving from its Old Testament foreshadowing to the spiritual reality of the sacrament and a critique of academic religious infighting.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Supper, Eucharist, Old Testament, New Testament, Types and Shadows, Sacramental Theology, Religious Conflict, Human Reason vs. Holy Spirit</keywords>

## Of the Foundation of the Old Testament; how this Testament was clearly shown in the Type among the Jews.

### I.

<margin>
A stifling or suffocating.
</margin>
When God sent *Moses* to *Pharaoh* and commanded him to let the children of *Israel* go into the wilderness and sacrifice to Him, and *Pharaoh* would not do it, then God sent great plagues and punishments upon him. Lastly, He sent the destroying angel, who slew all the *firstborn in Egypt*. There He commanded *Israel* the *Passover*, or to slay and eat the *Paschal Lamb*: specifically, *a lamb of a year old, and to sprinkle the doors and upper posts with its blood, so that the slaying anger should there* <margin>pass by</margin> *pass over and kill no one in the house* (Exodus 12). <note>original: Exod. 2. 12. The author likely intended Exodus 12.</note>

2. This was a *type* <note>A "type" is a person, event, or thing in the Old Testament believed to foreshadow something in the New Testament.</note> of the New Testament, showing how the posts and doors of our *life* should be sprinkled with the blood of the Lamb, *Christ*, so that God’s anger in soul and body may not swallow us up into His wrath. And just as they had to eat that lamb entirely and leave nothing remaining, so would the Lamb, *Christ*, give Himself in His Testament wholly as food for His Christendom, undivided. He would sprinkle the posts of our life with His blood of *love*, so that the destroying angel in God’s anger might not take hold of us and slay us.

3. We also have an image or likeness of this in the *unleavened cakes* which they were required to bake and eat. This showed that through this figure of the Paschal Lamb, which signified Christ, they should be a *new* and sweet <margin>dough</margin> <note>original: lump</note> when they ate the true Paschal Lamb, Christ, in His Testament. All of this was a figure of the *new regeneration* <note>The process of being "born again" or spiritually renewed.</note>, signifying how that rebirth would be born through the true Paschal Lamb, Christ (1 Corinthians 5:7), and how the New Birth would eat the sweet food of Divine Grace.

4. This is a powerful figure of the terrible Fall in *Adam*, and then of the New Birth or regeneration in *Christ*. For in *Adam*, the first birth—namely, the first angelic life—was slain by God’s anger; but by the Covenant of Grace in *Christ*, that same life was brought back again and spoken (or *inspired*) into *Adam* and *Eve*. Thus, the Spirit of God signifies by this figure of *Moses* in *Egypt* how that same "inspoken" grace should be fulfilled with heavenly and divine *substance*. It also shows how man should be <margin>smeared or streaked</margin> marked and sprinkled by that mark: namely—

<margin>
e Released or reconciled.
*Leviticus 8:14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21.
</margin>
5. We also see this image very clearly in the *sacrifices of Israel*: how they had to slay and sacrifice beasts and burn the fat. For when God gave the law to *Moses*, so that *Israel* should live in full obedience in the covenant of this law, He also gave him the figure <note>original: [ shewing ]</note> showing how this law should be fulfilled, and how man should be <gloss>e</gloss> atoned from sin and the anger of God; as we may clearly see this in the *third book of Moses in the eighth chapter* <note>Leviticus 8</note>, where the image of the reconciliation or atonement stands.

->Where it stands thus:<-

f Sacrifice.
g Cleansed it from sin.
</margin>
> <gloss>f</gloss> *And Moses caused a heifer to be brought for a sin offering, and Aaron with his sons laid their hands upon its head; there it was slain, and Moses took the blood, and with his finger put it all around the horns of the altar, and <gloss>g</gloss> cleansed the altar, and poured out the blood at the foot of the altar, and sanctified it, so that he made an atonement on it. And he took all the fat upon the innards, and the caul upon the liver, and the two kidneys with the fat on them, and burned it upon the altar. But the heifer with its hide, flesh, and dung, he burned with fire outside the camp, as the Lord had commanded him. And he brought a wether <note>A castrated ram.</note> or ram for a burnt sacrifice; and Aaron with his sons laid their hands upon its head, and it was slain there. And Moses sprinkled the blood all around the altar, and cut the ram into pieces, and burned the head, the pieces, and the rump. And he washed the innards and the legs with water, and so burned the whole ram upon the altar; this was a burnt sacrifice for a sweet aroma, a fire to the Lord as the Lord had commanded him (Leviticus 8:14, 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 21).*

6. This is a true figure of the sacrifice of Christ with our assumed humanity: how He has offered our humanity, through the sacrifice of His body, to the anger of God, and how God in this sacrifice has smelled His sweet love in the humanity of Christ and reconciled His wrath in the fire.

7. ->The figure of Christ stands thus:<-
