# Ch. I. showing how this Testament was in the Type.

and poured the anointing oil upon Aaron's head, and anointed him, so that he was sanctified.

7. Moses stands here in the figure of God, and *Aaron* stands in the figure of *Christ* according to our humanity; the *heifer* stands in the figure of the earthly, perished *Adam*, according to his bestial property; and the *ram* stands in the figure of the true *man* created in *Adam*, showing how that same man should be offered up to God again in the humanity of *Christ*. *Moses* anointed *Aaron*, which is to say: God anointed our humanity in *Christ* with His highest *love*, namely: with the *substantial wisdom* and divine substance in the name **JESUS**.

8. And that is why *Moses* first anointed the Tabernacle, which signifies the body of *Mary*, in which God became man; and therefore the angel called her *the blessed among all women* (*Luke 1:28*). For God first sanctified the Tabernacle, and sprinkled His anointing oil of love upon all the seven properties of the natural life. This life in the humanity of *Christ* was signified by the *Altar of Moses*, which God anointed when **JESUS** assumed man's life. The *laver* with its base signifies how God has anointed the human hand or *desire* in the humanity of *Christ*, with which He should do wonders. The anointed *head of Aaron* signifies how the humanity of *Christ*—namely, our human *science* <note>In this context, "science" refers to the human intellect, knowledge, or consciousness.</note>—should be anointed with the Holy Spirit.

9. God set this forth through *Moses* in the *type*; for when *Moses* had anointed *Aaron* along with the Tabernacle and the sons of *Aaron*, and established the whole ceremony, he then caused a heifer to be brought for a sin offering. This *heifer* signified the *coarse earthly man* impressed through *Adam's lust*. *Aaron* and his sons had to lay their hands upon the head of this heifer, which signifies how *God in Christ*, and then the priests or *Pharisees*, would lay their hands upon our *mortal humanity* assumed in *Christ*, and flay (that is, put to death) our humanity according to the substance of this world. It also signifies how His human blood should be sprinkled all around on the horns of the holy altar with the finger of God—namely, as with the laying hold of God's anger—and how God would thus cleanse His altar in humanity, upon which altar (the human *life*) holy sacrifices should be offered to God again.

10. But that *Moses* burned *the fat of the liver and kidneys* upon the altar signifies that our true Adamic *man from the slime* <note>original: "Limus," referring to the dust or clay from which Adam was created.</note> of the earth, according to its right *inward ground*, should not be cast away or thrust out from God, but be kindled in the fire of God's anger with the fire of *love*, and be offered to God. This was then done in the humanity of *Christ*, when He sacrificed our humanity to the anger of God, but yet did introduce it with His *love*

through Death into *Life*, as out of the fire there springs up a clear Light, as out of the dying fire, a new Life, which consists in power and Love.

<margin>i Note.</margin>
11. But that *Moses* did burn *with fire the Heifer with his hide, flesh, and dung, outside the host,* and only sprinkled the blood of the Heifer upon the horns of the Altar, and kindled the fat, it signifies, that the gross bestial body of our flesh with skin and bones, shall not come upon God's Altar, and inherit the kingdom of *God*, but it shall be consumed with the essential fire of the earth *outside the holy fire of God*: as *Moses* must burn the Heifer outside the host, so also should and must the gross bestial man be burnt up outside the City of God, namely: in its own principle, by that same nature-fire—as it is done before our eyes. But this earthly man's blood, in which the astral soul lives, should be sprinkled upon the horns of the Altar: which signifies the right man created in *Adam* out of the *Limus* <note>The "slime" or "dust" of the earth from which Adam was formed.</note> of the earth with the right *Astrum* <note>The "star" or astral influence governing the human soul and body.</note>, whose blood or power shall come again at the Last Day. That same blood was in Christ's humanity, with the inclusion of the heavenly blood, sprinkled upon the horns of the Altar in his suffering and dying on the tree of the Cross; for a sign, that our blood, after a right human manner, has with Christ come upon *God's Altar*, and that God in *Christ* has therewith marked us in the Eternal Heavenly Altar.

12. But that the other blood must be poured at the foot of the Altar, signifies, that our human blood, in which our outward human life consists, which here dies, is in its dying poured out at the foot of the Altar, namely: into the [four] elements, and reserved for the bringing again of the first true man, so that the right life shall be kept at the foot of the Altar—that is, in its own principle, as in *Mysterio Magno in Spiritu Mundi* <note>"In the Great Mystery in the Spirit of the World."</note>, in the Great Mystery in the Spirit of the world—until God shall purge this Altar of the four elements, and bring forth again the same pure virtue and power of the blood, together with the elements, and bring them into the Temperature <note>A state of perfect balance and harmony where no single element dominates.</note>, and so then the human blood shall again come to the soul, according to the property of the spiritual world.

13. For this cause must *Moses* and *Aaron* in the figure pour out the Heifer's blood at the foot of the Altar: for God was not concerned with the blood of beasts, but he set forth the *figure* of man's restoration, and signifies under it, that we men according to the outward gross earthly man were but such beasts, which gross bestial property had swallowed up the true man into itself. Thus he signifies, how he would with his power bring again and newly generate that same inward devoured ground. And as the power and virtue of our *Mumia* <note>The vital essence or life-force inherent in the physical body.</note>, of the oily property shall be kindled again through the fire of God, as *Moses* kindled the fat; so shall the power

# Ch. 1. showing how this Testament was in the Type:

of the inward ground, here even in the time of *this life*, be kindled with the divine fire, and burn upon God's Altar, which through the *unction* or anointing of the Spirit of God should be done *in us*: Even as *Moses* kindled the fat, so should also the spirit of Christ kindle our inward ground even in this [life's] time: And when this comes to be done, then should the heifer, namely: the beast-man be slain; that is, he should be daily *mortified*, and with his will and bestial desire be cast forth outside the host of God: for he is but earth and a beast, and *not fit for the kingdom of heaven*, John 6.

14. But the *ram* now signifies the right anointed man in the humanity of Christ, as also our inward ground, which with Christ’s anointing in us becomes living again. As God through *Aaron’s sons*, namely: *through the high priest*, would lay the hand of his anger upon *it*, and *slay it*, so that our human will might be *slain*; so shall in this flaying the blood of this *ram*, namely: *the right humanity*, be *sprinkled* everywhere upon the Altar of God, and the human will shall be split in pieces, and quite forsake its own will, and the *head*, namely: *the human senses or thoughts* be kindled with God’s fire: Even as *Moses* kindled the *head* of the *ram*, as also the *pieces* and the *rump*, so must also our humanity in Christ be *washed* with the *water of love*, and afterwards be sacrificed upon the Altar of God, namely: *on the Tree of the Cross*.

<margin>Note.</margin>
15. *Note*. But that the *ram* must be kindled with fire, and be offered to the Lord, *for a sweet savor to the Lord*: therein lies the *Great Mystery*: The *ram* is the right man created in *Adam*, which Christ, as the Word, or the power of God, took on him, from our Adamic humanity, in the body of *Mary*: and it signifies by the *ram*, that God in Christ would thus anoint our *right Adamic humanity*, with the holy anointing oil, and bring it to God again.

16. But seeing the human will had departed from God, and had brought itself into earthly *bestial lust*: therefore should this *ram*, namely: the humanity of Christ, be kindled with God's fire of anger: for the anger of God had become manifest in the humanity: and therefore God anointed first the humanity with the *Spirit of his love* in Christ’s humanity, and afterwards offered this *ram*, namely: the *right Adamic humanity* to the fire of God, that in the anointing of the *love-oil*, the anger-fire might be kindled, and the anger-fire in the anointing of the love, namely: through the oil of its holy substance, be transmuted, and wholly turned into a *love-burning*.

17. For our human soul had become an anger-fire, namely: an *enmity* against God, and therefore God thus reconciled it in the *love* of its substance: That is, God’s anger in the human life was thus reconciled, for God’s power *smelled*, or thus *drew*, in the fire, the human life again into the Eternal Word, namely: into God. <meta>This page continues the discussion of how God’s love interacts with human nature, specifically focusing on the spiritual mechanics of sacrifice as a precursor to Christ.</meta>

was the *Mouth*, which received the human life back into it, and swallowed up the averted human will into itself.

18. But when this Great Love had given itself up into the human life in Christ, then God’s "anger-fire"—with its devouring nature in the soul’s fire—was turned into mere Love, namely: wholly into a "love-fire." And thus *Death* in the fire of God’s anger, which holds the soul captive with its impression (wherein the soul was a darkness, or a dark fire), was broken open and turned again into the Light-Life.

19. And that is what God sets forth as a *Type* <note>original: "Type"; a prophetic symbol or foreshadowing</note> of the coming of Christ through this sacrifice. For *Moses* says: *This was a sweet savor to the Lord* <note>Exodus 29:18</note>. Now, God does not favor the bestial <note>animal-like or carnal</note> life, for that shall not inherit the Kingdom of God. God’s business which he conducted through *Moses* was not about the ram and the heifer; rather, he set the humanity represented by them before him. God’s imagination <note>divine creative thought or focus</note> went into the humanity through his "inspoken" <note>internally spoken or infused</note> Grace-Covenant—that is, in that "inspoken" Grace-word of Love which stood as a goal or limit in Man, even until the time of *Mary*, when it manifested itself in humanity. God’s imagination entered into this, and Man’s imagination entered into this *Type*—the sacrifice in the fire. Thus, the incorporated Grace-Covenant also went with Man’s imagination into the sacrifice of the fire, as a *Type* signifying how Christ should reconcile God’s anger within humanity.

20. And thus was the human will ransomed in the fire through the Type of Christ. For the incorporated Grace-Covenant went together with the human desire—expressed through their *prayer*, which pressed into God by means of this sacrifice in the fire—into the fire of the sacrifice. There, it reconciled (that is, destroyed) the earthly, bestial property of the human will in the fire with Love. For with the fire, God formed an image before him as a substance; in this fire-substance, God’s fire imaged itself and reconciled the elementary fire <note>the physical/energetic life-force</note> in Man. His eternal love-fire *favored* the human will through the incorporated Grace, using the *medium* of the fire; for *Moses* had holy fire with which he kindled the sacrifice.

21. Thus was the elementary fire of Man reconciled in the Holy Fire. Yet a bestial *medium*—namely, the flesh of beasts—had to enter the Holy Fire of *Moses*, seeing as Man had become bestial. This was so that the bestial nature might be burned away in the Holy Fire through the Father’s anger-fire, and so that God’s love-fire might kindle the human soul-fire in its introduced desire within the sacrifice. And so, God’s desire in the Word *smelled* Man’s desire through the fire; for in the fire, the bestial vanity of Man’s will burned away in the anger-fire. Then the clear human will pressed into God’s love-fire as a *sweet savor*; for the incorporated, paradisical, "inspoken" Grace pressed into God along with the clear will of Man.

<summary>The author explains the spiritual significance of Mosaic animal sacrifices as a symbolic "Type" of Christ, where the "anger-fire" of God consumes the bestial nature of man to allow the human soul to reunite with God's "love-fire."</summary>
<keywords>Sacrifice, Moses, Christ, Type, Anger-fire, Love-fire, Grace-Covenant, Atonement, Bestial nature, Alchemy of the soul</keywords>

--- Page 109 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Old Testament sacrifices served as a "type" or symbolic precursor to the New Testament sacrament of Christ's body and blood.</meta>

22. And that is what *Moses* says: *It was a sacrifice of a sweet savor unto the Lord*. For God desired to smell nothing there but human will—specifically, the human life which existed in the Word of God before the world began. This life existed without a physical body <note>original: "without Creature"</note> but was present in power, which was then breathed into the created image of man. God "smelled" that same life through the sacrifice in the *essence* <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for the fundamental substance or being of a thing</note> of Christ—that is, through the spoken promise of grace. He reconciled the turned-away human will through grace in the fire, so that the human will became divine again. He brought (1) the fire of human life and (2) the fire of God’s love into one fire, as if into *one burning life*. That was a true sacrifice of reconciliation, or a sin-offering, where sin was offered to the fire of God’s anger to be consumed.

23. In this way, the animal flesh—which they offered and later ate—was also sanctified for man, because God’s imagination <note>In this context, "imagination" refers to God's creative focus or spiritual influence entering into the physical world.</note> in the Covenant entered into it. Therefore, *Moses* calls it *Holy Flesh*; it was also called *Holy Bread*, such as the *showbread* <note>The twelve loaves of bread placed in the Jewish Temple as an offering; see 1 Samuel 21:6 and Matthew 12:4.</note>. All of this stood as a "type" or symbol, signifying how that same indwelling power of the Covenant of Grace would manifest itself with heavenly substance in Christ. It would be given as food for the human soul—specifically for the "fire" of the soul—and in that food, the soul's fire would be transformed into a fire of love.

<margin>Note.</margin>
24. For in the Old Testament, this sweet Covenant of Grace gave itself as food into the sacrifice of the fiery desire of the soul, acting as the soul's mouth within the fire. The soul—that is, the mouth of the soul's faith—did eat of this sweet grace in the fire of sacrifice. They did *not* eat it in physical substance, but in power, looking forward to the fulfillment that was to come, until that power was manifested in the flesh. Meanwhile, their physical bodies ate the blessed bread and flesh, which also contained the power of grace (the spiritual influence of the Covenant). Thus, the *Jews* ate Christ’s flesh and drank his blood in the *essence* of grace—in power and in symbol—at a time when that power was not yet flesh and blood. Nevertheless, that same Word of Grace, which later became man, was present within it.

25. But when the time was fulfilled and that same indwelling Word of Grace (which the soul received through the desire of faith in the fiery essence) *became man*, it then gave itself as food to this "mouth of faith"—the fiery essence of the soul—according to both its divine and human properties. We will write more extensively about this in the following sections and establish the true foundation of how *Christ’s flesh and blood are to be eaten and drunk*. This will not be based on mere imagination or opinion, but on the foundation of Scripture and certain knowledge through God’s grace.

<summary>The author explains that Old Testament sacrifices were symbolic "types" where the faithful spiritually consumed Christ's grace through the "fire" of their souls before he took human form. This establishes a theological link between ancient Jewish rituals and the Christian sacrament of the Eucharist.</summary>

<header>Ch. 2.</header>

### The Second Chapter.

## Of the Supper of the New Testament,
## how the Type has become Substance.

->I.<-

<margin>a Or, unmolded.</margin>
When the time was *fulfilled* that this Covenant of Grace should manifest itself and take on humanity with soul and body, then the *type* ceased; for the anger-fire of God in the soul and in the flesh of man was together *<sup>a</sup> imprinted* in the substantial Word—that is, in the divine *Ens*. This divine *Ens*, out of the divine power, also took on the divine *Ens* of Adam, which had vanished regarding the kingdom of heaven (that is, regarding the holy working fire) when the soul *imaged* itself into *earthliness*. It made it living—that is, burning in itself again—through the holy fire, where the divine sacrifice in the fire was then manifested in humanity.

2. For the soul was of the Father's property, according to the power of his fire, and had become a mere fire of anger. *This the Father gave to the Son*, as the manifested *Covenant of Grace*, and brought the Father's anger-fire in the soul into the Son's love-fire, so that the Father's anger-fire and the Son's love-fire stood in one *ground* within the human substance. The love-fire became substantial—that is, a heavenly flesh—and gave itself to the anger-fire of the Father in the property of the human *life's* property as food for a burning fire, in the same way that a man puts a pleasant *oily balsam* into a fire; and then the fire, in its burning, gives forth from itself a pleasant smell and virtue from this balsam oil.

3. Thus the Father now *smelled this pleasant savor* in humanity within the soul's fire, a savor which he formerly *smelled in the sacrifice in the type*. For Christ was now the true high priest who offered to God his Father the acceptable sacrifice of the sweet savor in humanity and reconciled the anger in humanity. The altar of God upon which *Moses* sacrificed was now in the humanity of Christ, who offered up to God the sweet humanity from the heavenly *Ens*, along with the *Adamic* humanity, into his anger-fire, which burned in the Adamic humanity. Sin in the Adamic flesh was destroyed through the sweet heavenly flesh—that is, through the substantial Word of love, through *JESUS*, that is, God's greatest sweetness.

4. God brought the sacrifice of *Moses*—that is, the reconciliation—into

5. And we see this preeminently in the Supper of *Christ*, which, when at last he would enter into his sufferings and kill sin in our humanity with the substantial love-fire in his blood, then at last he ate the *Paschal Lamb* with his disciples. For he had given himself up into the type with his Grace-Covenant, with the heavenly flesh, and would fulfill the type with the flesh of love and bring forth the Adamic humanity through death—namely, through God's anger, through the inclusion of the Adamic life into God's love-fire—and break open the *prison of death*.

6. So now, in the type of the Paschal Lamb, he began the New Testament—namely, the *fulfilling* of these types—and led his disciples to the sacrifice of the New Testament; that is, to the altar of God, in his flesh and blood, so that they should eat the fulfillment of the Old Testament in the sacrifice of the New Testament. For he brought the Old Testament—that is, the *figure*—into the *New*—that is, into his flesh and blood, which was the *sacrifice of reconciliation for the sins of the world*; and gave them the <margin>b Or, reconciliation.</margin> atonement in his flesh to eat, and in his blood to drink.

7. For the reconciliation should no longer be done in fire, where the Father's anger burned away the vanity in man's faith, but it should be done in the love-fire, in the *flesh of Christ*. They should now with their faith's desire—namely, with the soul's fiery life's mouth—eat and drink the reconciliation with Christ's flesh and blood: *no longer* with faith in the type, but in substance; no longer in power without substance, but with substantial divine and human power, wherein the *humanity* of *Christ himself* should be the Paschal Lamb: not deity without humanity, but *deity and humanity alike*.

8. For the *Paschal Lamb* must be eaten entirely, and so he also would not give them only a piece of his humanity, but <note>Note</note> gave himself to them wholly and altogether, into their soul's fire-mouth—namely, into the faith's desire. The soul's fiery mouth was now the fire of God which devoured the sacrifice, just as common fire devours oil and gives a clear light out of its consuming. Thus also Christ gave to his disciples—that is, to their faith's desire, meaning to the fiery mouth of the soul as to the true life—his heavenly body and his heavenly blood, in that manner and way as the heavenly *Ens* in the limit of the covenant gave itself into *Mary*, into the vanished *Ens*, and became one person, wholly undivided.

9. Note: He gave his *whole* heavenly humanity to them, into their Adamic humanity—meaning to the *paradisiacal ground*. For every life desires an *Ens* of its likeness—namely, every fire desires

10. *The foolish fire-life* had become dark through the *impression of death*, and therefore needed a *light Ens* from the *Divine Light*, as a *Substance of Light*: as a *holy anointing oil*: wherein the dark, painful *soul's fire* changes itself into a *light- and love-desire*. This was now conceived upon *Christ's Altar*, namely: out of *Christ's humanity*, the *Substantial Love*, namely: the *Substantial Wisdom of God*. That *same fire* in this *substance* was the *Name* and the power *JESUS*, namely: *God's greatest holiness*, which changes the *soul's fire* into a *light*. For this reason, *Christ* said: *He was the light of the world, John 8:12*. For in his power, the *soul* has again attained eyes of *Divine Light*, so that it beholds *God* again.

11. And as a *man* gives *oil* to a *common fire*, from which arises a *light*, so in like manner it is to be understood concerning the *dark soul's fire*; which receives into itself *Christ's heavenly Ens* of the *Divine Substantial Love* in this *Testament*, and kindles again the vanished, even *heavenly Ens* in *Adam*, and burns in its likeness. In this, the *Kingdom of Heaven*, namely: the *City of God*, is again unlocked, where the *Divine Light* in that *same* unlocked *Paradisiacal Ground* shines again: which is the *City of God in man*, where the *Holy Spirit* dwells; of which *Christ* says: *He that eats my flesh and drinks my blood, he continues in me and I in him, John 6:56.*

12. In this *unlocked Paradisiacal Ground*, *Christ*, namely: the *Divine Love-Ens*, continues dwelling in the *light*. For the power in the *light* is the *Divine Ens*, namely: the *supernatural heavenly flesh*, a *Tincture of Life*, which *tincture* tinctures the *soul* and gives the *heavenly fire-glance* to the *soul's fire*. But yet, so that a *man* may rightly understand it: the *soul* is the *Father's fire*, which receives into itself (namely: into its fiery desire) the *Son's Substantial Love*, namely: the *holy love-fire*, which has turned itself into the *human tincture*, namely: into *flesh* and *blood*—understand, into that *same human life's power*. *Thus is the changing of the soul's will effected.*

13. Thus we see first this figure in *Moses' sacrifice* of *reconciliation*: that the *soul*, namely: the *faith's desire* of the *soul*, brought itself into the *sacrifice* as into a *substance*, and with the *substance* went into the *fire*, where the *reconciliation through fire* was made in the introduced *Covenant of the love-fire*. This was done in the manner and way that the *Substantial Love of Christ* should give itself up into the *soul's fire* (wherein *God's anger* burned) for food and *reconciliation*. Secondly, we see also by *Moses*, not only how the *soul* is ransomed from sin through *fire*, and with the *love* in the *Covenant* became fed with the *holy fire of grace*, but they also blessed the *bread* and *flesh* through the reconciliation of the *Grace-Covenant*,

and did eat the same, namely: the holy bread and flesh, as God also commanded them.

14. This signifies the oral eating and drinking of the Substantial Grace; where not only the soul is fed, but (Note) also the true Adamical man <note>original: "Adamicall Man"; referring to the original, uncorrupted human nature created in Adam</note>, *who shall rise from death again*. For the earth was cursed through man’s sin, and man was forced by that curse of God’s anger to eat of the earthly fruit. But with this sacrifice and blessing of the Covenant, God now establishes *a new blessing*, so that *Israel* ate of blessed food again, where Grace pressed through the curse once more, just as the sun presses and penetrates through water.

15. All of this was a *type* <note>original: "Type"; a biblical person, event, or ritual that foreshadows a future reality in Christ</note> of the Testament of Christ, which would not only feed the soul with substantial, fiery love, but (Note) also the true Adamical body. This was signified when he ordained his New Testament under the forms of bread and wine: it showed how the inward grace of love would press through the outward man, just as the sun penetrates through water, or as fire penetrates through iron.

16. The oral eating and drinking of his New Testament is signified by the Passover Lamb <note>original: "Paschal Lamb"</note> in the Old Testament. In that rite, the Grace-Covenant of the divine blessing gave itself into a visible, physical substance—namely, into a *medium* through which the human body received grace. This points toward the New Testament, where Christ—the Substantial Grace—would, with his love and the power of his flesh and blood (possessing both divine and human properties), *give himself* to his fellow-members, the Christians. He does this through a physical *medium* of blessed bread and wine for food and drink. Through this, the accursed body made from the *slime* <note>original: "Limus"; the dust or clay of the earth from which Adam was formed</note> of the earth (which has swallowed the curse into itself) is blessed once again.

17. We see this clearly in the Last Supper of Christ. With the institution of the New Testament, Christ did not intend to ordain any *strange* or *new thing*, but only to fulfill the Old Testament. With the Substantial Grace, which had become MAN in his soul and flesh, he gave himself up into the Covenant of the Old Testament. He himself became the *fulfillment*—namely, the Passover Lamb and the holy bread and flesh through which our true Adamical man is to be blessed. *For he first ate the Passover Lamb with his disciples and incorporated himself with them in the Covenant of the Old Testament, and washed their feet for them. Afterward, he took the bread, gave thanks, broke it, and gave it to them, saying: "Take and eat; this is my body, which is given for you." In the same way, he also took the cup after the supper, gave thanks, gave it to them, and said: "Drink all of you from it; this is my blood of the New Testament, which is shed for many for the forgiveness of sins. I say to you, I will from now on drink no more of this..."*

It emphasizes that the New Testament fulfills rather than replaces the Old Covenant through the physical medium of bread and wine.</summary>

*fruit of the Vine, till at the day that I will drink it new with you in my Fathers Kingdom, Matth. 26. Mark. 14. I Cor. 11. 23.*

18. This is now the true ground, and it signifies, first, how the sweet grace of God's Covenant has given itself up into our humanity; and secondly, how that same Grace-Covenant (which in the Old Testament, with the sacrifices and Paschal Lamb, gave itself to *Israel* under that same food for a blessing) does now, with the assumed humanity, give itself to man also through a *medium*, namely: under bread and wine, to be eaten and drunk. And thirdly: how he would not take away the first Covenant and begin some new thing; but how he *himself* would be that very Grace-Covenant, and now present himself as a man, and now give himself into man after a divine and human way. Just as they had participated in him before, with his grace in the Covenant and in the sacrifice in a spiritual way *in power*, through a *medium*, so now they should participate in him also in a corporeal and spiritual way through the *medium* of bread and wine, and substantially participate in that same incorporated grace in the Covenant.

19. For the reason was this: that he would give himself to them to be participated in both a human and also in a divine property alike; that they should participate in the grace (which had given itself into the humanity, and slain Death, and opened the human life again, and brought it through Death) in a new human life. So that the same new life out of Christ's death and resurrection might unlock even their life which was shut up in death, he therefore gave them this new unlocked human life of his assumed human life, with his flesh and blood, even under an *elementary medium*, so that faith might conceive it through a *medium*.

20. Therefore, there must be a medium, so that (first) God's imagination <note>In this context, "imagination" refers to a creative, formative, or directed spiritual power or desire</note> of his love, and (secondly) man's faith's desire might come together and take hold of one another by a *medium*. For as the participation of the Old Testament was done in a spiritual way, so now it should be both in a substantial and spiritual way alike. For the Deity has now presented the Paschal Lamb in the humanity of Christ; and God's imagination (which went formerly into the sacrifice) went now into the humanity of Christ. And the imagination of the humanity of Christ went with divine power into the *medium* of bread and wine, and through the same, in the participation of it, into the human life.

Ch. 3.

## The Third Chapter.

# How the Disciples of Christ have Eaten and Drunk Christ's flesh and blood: and how that is properly to be understood.

### I.

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'R' featuring a floral and vine motif within a square frame.</image-desc>

Reason should here go out from the image-likeness into that which is unlike any image, and forsake the *folly* for which men strive; for it was not a participation in an image, but is understood under an image-like one. Christ did not give his disciples his imaged, creaturely, outward, palpable, fleshy humanity as some piece of it. No, that is not consistent, for *he sat by them at the table* and did not tear the imaged substance of his body; but he gave them the spiritual humanity, namely: the power and virtue of his body and blood, his own *Mumia*, in which the divine and human power is understood. This *Mumia* is a true human substance of flesh and blood, and is a spiritual flesh out of which the visible image grows, and it is wholly one with the visible image.

2. Observe a similarity in the sun, which stands in its orb, but presses with its luster, power, and whole substance—with all that it is in essence, power, and substance—forth out of itself into the whole *world*, and gives itself to every element, substance, and creature, to all herbs and trees, and also to every creaturely life, and works in all things, whatever will receive it. And yet it does not tear itself apart when it goes forth from itself and gives itself to the other creatures; it remains always whole and does not depart from its substance.

3. So also is the Supper of Christ to be understood, as we see a similarity of it in the herbs and grass of the earth, which neither do, nor can do, anything *without* the power of the sun. The herb sets the jaws of its essence wide open toward the sun’s power and virtue; that is, it hungers only after that, and the sun shines into that hunger and kindles *the spirit*, namely: the sulfur, salt, and oil of the herb. As soon as that is done, so that the sun’s virtue finds or perceives itself in the sulfur, salt, and oil of the herb, then it *works* therein. Whereby the herb attains a perceptible warmth, and conceives and impresses the sun’s virtue into itself, so that the sun’s virtue and power become *substantial* in the herb, and grow with the herb, and tincture it, whereby the herb becomes sunny or solar, and works toward [<insert>the bringing forth of</insert>] fruit.

<meta>Catchword: 4. So</meta>

<summary>This chapter explains that Christ's presence in the Eucharist is not a physical tearing of his body, but a participation in his "Mumia" or spiritual humanity. This process is compared to how the sun imparts its essence and power to plants to make them grow without the sun itself being diminished or divided.</summary>

*...fruit of the vine, until that day that I will drink it new with you in my Father's Kingdom. Matthew 26, Mark 14, 1 Corinthians 11:23.*

18. This is now the true foundation, and it signifies, first, how the sweet grace of God's Covenant has given itself up into our humanity. Secondly, it shows how that same Grace-Covenant (which in the Old Testament gave itself to Israel as a blessing through sacrifices and the Passover Lamb <note>original: "Paschal-Lamb"</note>) now gives itself to humanity through the assumed human nature of Christ. This happens through a medium <note>original: "Medium"; an intermediate substance or means of communication</note>—namely, under the forms of bread and wine—to be eaten and drunk. Thirdly, it shows how he would not take away the first Covenant and begin something entirely new; rather, he *himself* would be that very Grace-Covenant. He now presents himself as a man, giving himself to humanity in both a divine and human way. Just as people had participated in him previously through his grace in the Covenant and the sacrifices in a spiritual way *in power* through a medium, they should now also participate in him in both a physical <note>original: "corporeall"</note> and spiritual way through the medium of bread and wine, and substantially participate in that same grace which is now embodied <note>original: "incorporated"</note> in the Covenant.

19. The reason for this was that he wanted to give himself to them so they could participate in both his human and divine natures alike. He intended for them to participate in the Grace—which had entered into humanity, conquered <note>original: "slaine"</note> Death, reopened the human life, and brought it through Death—within a new human life. He gave them this new, unlocked human life (derived from his own assumed human life, with his flesh and blood) even under an elementary medium <note>the physical elements of bread and wine</note>, so that Faith might grasp it through that medium. This was done so that the same New Life arising from Christ's Death and Resurrection might unlock their own lives, which were previously imprisoned in Death.

20. Therefore, there must be a medium so that, first, God’s "imagination" <note>In this context, "imagination" refers to God's creative will or the internal forming of his love</note> of his love and, second, the desire of human faith might come together and take hold of one another through that medium. Just as the participation in the Old Testament was done in a spiritual way, it should now be done in both a substantial and spiritual way *alike*. For the Deity has now presented the Passover Lamb within the humanity of Christ. God’s "imagination" (which formerly went into the animal sacrifices) now went into the humanity of Christ; and the "imagination" of Christ’s humanity went with divine power into the medium of bread and wine. Through the participation in those elements, that power enters *into human life*.

<header>Ch. 3.</header>

## *The Third Chapter.*

### How the Disciples of Christ have eaten and drunk Christ's flesh and blood: and how that is properly to be understood.

->I.<-

<image-desc>Decorative drop cap 'R' set in a square frame with a floral pattern.</image-desc>
Reason should here go out from the image-likeness into that which is un-image-like, and forsake the folly for which men strive; for it was not an image-like participation, but is understood under an image-like one. Christ did not give his disciples his imaged, creaturely, outward, palpable, fleshly humanity as some piece of it: No, that is not possible, for *he sat by them at the table*, and did not tear the imaged substance of his body. Instead, he gave them the spiritual humanity, *namely:* the power and virtue of his body and blood, his own <term>Mumia</term>, in which the divine and human power is understood. This <term>Mumia</term> is a true human substance of flesh and blood and is a spiritual flesh, out of which the visible image grows, and is wholly one with the visible image.

2. See an analogy in the *sun*, which stands in its orb, but presses with its luster, power, and whole substance—with everything that it is in essence, power, and substance—forth out of itself into the *whole world*. It gives itself to every element, substance, and creature, to all *herbs and trees*, and also to every creaturely life, and *works* in all things, whatever will receive it; and yet it does not tear itself when it goes forth from itself and gives itself to the other *creatures*. It remains always whole and does not go away from its substance.

3. So also is the Supper of Christ to be understood; as we see an analogy of it in the herbs and grass of the earth, which neither do, nor can do anything *without* the power of the *sun*. The herb sets the jaws of its essence wide open toward the *sun's* power and virtue; that is, it hungers only after that, and into that hunger the *sun* shines and kindles *the spirit*, *namely:* the <term>Brimstone</term>, <term>Salt</term>, and <term>Oil</term> of the herb. And as soon as that is done, so that the *sun's* virtue finds or perceives itself in the <term>Brimstone</term>, <term>Salt</term>, and <term>Oil</term> of the herb, then it *works* within it. From this, the herb attains a perceptible warmth and conceives and impresses the *sun's* virtue into itself, so that the *sun's* virtue and power becomes substantial in the herb and grows with the herb, and tinctures it, whereby the herb becomes *sunny* or *Solar*, and works toward [bringing forth] fruit.

fruit of the *Vine, until that day when I will drink it new with you in my Father's Kingdom*, Matthew 26, Mark 14, 1 Corinthians 11:23.

18. This is now the true foundation, and it signifies, first, how the sweet grace of God's Covenant has given itself up into our humanity. Secondly, it shows how that same Grace-Covenant (which in the Old Testament gave itself to *Israel* as a blessing through sacrifices and the Paschal Lamb <note>The Passover lamb sacrificed by the Israelites</note>) now gives itself to man through the assumed humanity <note>The human nature taken on by Christ</note> under a *medium* <note>original: "Medium"; an intermediate substance or means</note>—namely, under bread and wine—to be eaten and drunk. He now presents himself as a man and gives himself *into* man in both a divine and human way. Just as they had participated in him previously through his grace in the Covenant and the sacrifice in a spiritual way in power, they should now also participate in him in both a physical and spiritual way through the *medium* of bread and wine, and substantially participate in that same grace incorporated in the Covenant.

19. For the reason was this: he wanted to give himself to them so they could participate in both a human and a divine property alike. He intended for them to participate in the grace (which had entered into humanity, destroyed Death, reopened human life, and brought it through Death) within a new human life. He did this so that the same New Life, arising out of Christ’s Death and Resurrection, might unlock their own life which was imprisoned in death. Therefore, he gave them this new, unlocked human life from his own assumed human life, along with his flesh and blood, even under an *elementary medium* <note>A physical substance belonging to the material world</note>, so that Faith might conceive it through a *medium*.

20. Therefore, there must be a *medium* so that, first, God’s imagination <note>In this context, "imagination" refers to the divine creative will or the internal forming of a spiritual reality</note> of his love and, second, man’s desire of faith might come together and take hold of one another through a *medium*. For just as the participation in the Old Testament was done in a spiritual way, it should now be done in both a substantial and spiritual way alike. The Deity has now presented the Paschal Lamb in the humanity of Christ; and God’s imagination (which formerly went into the sacrifice) now went into the humanity of Christ. The imagination of the humanity of Christ went with divine power into the *medium* of bread and wine, and through that medium—during the participation in it—*into human life*.

<summary>This page explains that the Lord's Supper is the fulfillment of the Old Testament Covenant, providing a physical and spiritual "medium" (bread and wine) through which Christ's resurrected life enters and transforms human life.</summary>
<keywords>Eucharist, Covenant, Medium, Humanity of Christ, Paschal Lamb, Resurrection, Faith, Sacramental Theology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the nature of Christ's body and the spiritual mechanics of the Last Supper.</meta>

### Chapter 3: How the Disciples of Christ have Eaten and Drunk Christ’s flesh and blood: and how that is properly to be understood.

-> I. <-

Reason should here depart from the physical image into that which is beyond images, and forsake the *folly* for which men strive. For it was not a physical participation, though it is understood through a physical one. Christ did not give his disciples his physical, creaturely, outward, palpable, fleshy humanity, as if giving a piece of it. No, that is not logical, for he sat by them at the table and did not tear the physical substance of his body. Instead, he gave them the spiritual humanity—namely, the power and virtue of his body and blood, his own <term>Mumia</term> <note>original: "Mumia"; in Paracelsian thought, this refers to the vital essence or spiritual "balsam" of a living body that carries its life-force</note>, in which the Divine and human power is understood. This <term>Mumia</term> is a true human substance of flesh and blood, yet it is a spiritual flesh from which the visible image grows, and it is wholly *one* with the visible image.

2. Consider a comparison in the Sun, which remains in its orbit but presses forth out of itself into the *whole world* with its luster, power, and whole substance—with everything that it is in essence, power, and substance. It gives itself to every element, substance, and creature—to all herbs and trees, and also to every living creature. It works in all things, whatever will receive it, and yet it does not tear itself apart when it goes forth from itself. Even as it gives itself to other creatures, it always remains whole and does not depart from its own substance.

3. The Supper of Christ is to be understood in the same way. We see a comparison of it in the herbs and grass of the earth, which neither do, nor can do, anything *without* the power of the Sun. The plant sets the "jaws" of its essence wide open toward the Sun’s power and virtue; that is, it hungers only after that. Into that hunger, the Sun shines and kindles *the Spirit*—namely, the sulfur, salt, and oil <note>In alchemy, these "Three Principles" (Sulphur, Salt, and Mercury/Oil) were believed to be the fundamental components of all matter</note> of the herb. As soon as this is done, and the Sun’s virtue finds or perceives itself within the sulfur, salt, and oil of the herb, it then *works* within it. From this, the herb attains a perceptible warmth; it conceives and impresses the Sun’s virtue into itself, so that the Sun’s virtue and power become *substantial* in the herb. It grows with the herb and tints it, whereby the herb becomes "solar" or sun-like, and works toward <insert>the bringing forth</insert> of fruit.

<summary>The author explains that the Eucharist involves partaking of Christ's spiritual "Mumia" rather than his physical flesh, using the Sun's influence on plants as an analogy for how divine substance inhabits earthly forms.</summary>
<keywords>Eucharist, Mumia, spiritual humanity, Sun analogy, alchemy, sulfur salt and oil, divine substance, Last Supper</keywords>

--- Page 120 ---
4. In the same way, we are to understand Christ’s Testaments. For He is the Sun of Life and the *Light of the world* <margin>John 8:12</margin>. According to His divinity, He is the Eternal Speaking Word—that is, the power and essence of the Godhead and the power of the Divine Light. According to His humanity, He is the formed, outspoken Word, which is entirely one with the Eternal Speaking Word. He has introverted our received humanity with the Eternal Word; he has turned the outward inward (the Substance of Time) and turned the inward outward (the Substance of Eternity). With the Divine Substance, through the human substance He received from us, He has become "pressing forth." Just as the sun presses forth from itself and gives of itself, so the Divine Substance gives itself forth through the human. <note>Note: The Divine Substance brings the human forth along with itself; for the Divine Desire moves toward the human essence, and the human essence moves toward the Divine "out-pressing" delight or desire.</note>

5. <note>Note: Because the Divine power and virtue excel the human, the human is brought forth through the Divine power. Thus, the human power and virtue give themselves together with the Divine, through the Divine power. The human power and virtue are a perception or discovery of the Divine, wherein the Divine finds itself in something—namely, in the formed substance of the Word of Power. In this, the Divine power loves itself as its own perceptible substance, just as the soul loves itself in its body.</note>

6. <note>Note: In this way, God has poured His love through the human substance in the humanity of Christ into Christ’s disciples. This happened in the same manner as the sun’s power in the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: *in Spiritu Mundi*</note>—as it mingles with the outflowing power of the stars and the *spiritual* sulfur, salt, and oil in the upper elements. The sun captures their power in its brilliance and gives itself to them, while their power gives itself into the sun’s power, and so they work together. By this, the light of Nature is unlocked. Through this working, they give themselves into the earth, herbs, trees, and all creatures, so that such a virtue exists within a plant or tree. From this, the growth or budding of creatures arises. In such growth or life, one must always understand two things: (1) an elementary, physical power, and (2) a super-elementary, solar, and astral <note>term: starry or celestial</note> power, where the solar and astral press forth with their strong power alongside the elementary.</note>

7. <note>Note: Now we see in herbs and all things that grow out of the—</note>

<summary>This page explains the mystical union of Christ’s divine and human natures, using the sun as an analogy for how divine substance "presses forth" into the physical world. It describes how Christ’s love was poured into His disciples through a spiritual-chemical process involving the "Light of Nature" and the elements.</summary>

# Chapter 3: How Christ's Disciples fed on his flesh and blood.

...the Earth. When plants take hold of and eat the power and virtue of the sun and stars within them, they do not tear themselves apart or use a separate, physical mouth to do it. Instead, the essence of their desire—namely, the natural sulfur <note>original: "Brimstone"</note>, salt, and oil within the herb—acts as the *mouth*. This mouth takes hold of and eats the influence of the upper elements of the sun and stars into itself. In this same way, it is to be understood concerning Christ's disciples *and all other people: they have eaten and drunk Christ's flesh and blood* under the form of bread and wine, as if through a *medium*, using the essential mouth of a desiring faith. This is not done with a limited, physical grasp, but with the desire of faith.

8. **Note:** Yet, so that a person may rightly understand this, he must know: 
(1) The outward mouth, with its physical grasp, takes in the outward elementary substances of bread and wine. The mortal, temporary substance of man—that is, the *gross flesh* (which shall not and cannot inherit the Kingdom of Heaven, *John 6:63; 1 Corinthians 15:50*)—takes in and eats the bread and wine. For bread and wine are also earthly, temporary substances which go into the belly and are then expelled, like all natural food (*Matthew 15:17*). 

(2) The *true, original human*, who was created in *Adam*—who, before the animal-like coarseness of the fall, was a true human and an image of the visible world according to its inward spiritual foundation—that same human eats Christ's flesh. This is a spiritual, substantial flesh, eaten with the *essential desire*. 

**Note:** Understand that this is the flesh he took upon himself in Mary—namely, our *human* flesh—but not the outward foundation of the physical four elements. Rather, it is the inward holy element: the foundation from which the four elements proceed. It is not fire, air, water, and earth, but the eternal pure element, which is a substance of Paradise. This substance is hidden within the four elements. Understand it as the substance of eternity, from which time emerged into a <margin>visible</margin> substance. That same flesh is like a spiritual *mumia* <note>A term used in alchemy and Paracelsian medicine to describe the vital essence or "mummy" of a body that contains its life-force.</note> from which the visible body of man took its origin *by the word "Let it be"* <note>original: "in verbo Fiat"</note>. In *Adam*, this substance became blind to the Kingdom of Heaven when he turned his desire toward the coarseness of the four elements. This fleshly substance was *filled with heavenly living substance and made living again* when Christ became man. 

**Note:** That is what the true Adamic human eats, which remains hidden within the coarse shell of the four elements. And in that substance, the true human *eats Christ's passion, suffering, and death*. Through his victory, Christ's death has become an eternal life. This life, having risen out of the death of Christ, breaks our earthly evil will and has become a *death to our death*—that death which our outward nature mistakenly considers to be life.

9. **Note:** And here Christ's passion and dying are put on...

This name has become a substantial power and virtue—namely, the supernatural wisdom of God—in which the *whole Deity* is understood to be active.

11. The soul's mouth eats of this holy substance, by which the soul is turned into a burning fire of love; for the love of Christ gives *Ens* <note>being or essence</note> and substance to the soul's fire. The fiery life of the soul takes this love-substance into its essence, and through this burning of love, the temple of ᵇ *Soph-JAH* is unlocked again—namely, the substantial, holy, and powerful light, the true spirit which vanished in *Adam*. Just as a light comes forth out of fire, so through the soul's fire, another principle—namely, the property of the *divine power and virtue*—goes forth. In that outgoing power and virtue dwells the supernatural, non-substantial <note>immaterial or beyond physical substance</note> God in Trinity. For this is the *divine principle* where God dwells, works, and wills within man, in which the <margin>ᶜ Penetrates.</margin> *divine light* is understood; this light ᶜ diffuses itself through the true man, just as fire does through iron.

12. Note: Understand it correctly in this way: Man exists in *three principles* or *three beginnings*. (1) The *visible man* is the visible world, which is both outward and inward—that is, transitory and non-transitory, consisting of the pure element and its outflow, the four elements. (2) The *gross man*, with outward flesh and blood, is the body made of the four elements. The spiritual body in the *quintessence* (or fifth essence) is the holy element hidden within the four, which is neither hot nor cold, nor dry nor moist. But the *foundation of the soul* is not that element; it is grounded in the *Eternal Word*, where the fiery Word of the Father’s property breathed itself into the created body as another principle of an eternal nature.

13. Now, the third (3) *supernatural foundation* of man is the true spirit—meaning the *substantial light* and *substantial wisdom*—which entered man when the soul was breathed in. However, it vanished when the soul turned itself away from the breathing Word and toward its own will and visible substances with its desire. Because the soul’s fiery life (originating from the Father’s property) could no longer reach that light, this foundation vanished as far as the creature was concerned—not within God, but only for the creaturely soul.

...and wine, and receive it into the earthly mortal mouth, I would think that I comprehended Christ's flesh and blood with my fleshly mouth, just as I comprehend bread and wine with it.

22. No: that cannot be. Bread and wine are but a *medium* or means for it, even as the earthly mouth is a *medium* or means for the spirit. The spiritual-faith-mouth of man comprehends Christ's flesh and blood under bread and wine, not as *inherent* in bread and wine; for bread and wine do not change themselves into Christ's flesh and blood, but they are an ordained means. This *medium* belongs to the visible man, through which *medium* the invisible gives itself into the invisible spiritual man.

<margin>f By bread only.</margin>
23. In bread and wine two properties are understood: (1) the gross elementary earthly substance, which belongs to the mortal man; and then (2) the power and virtue within them, in which the tincture of the bread and the wine lies. This (*tincture*) is above the elementary substance, in which the four elements lie in the temperature <note>A state of balance or harmony among the elements</note>, which is a heavenly, paradisiacal power and virtue. That same tincture of bread and wine is the true *medium* by which Christ, the human tincture, gives himself into the human life; for *man does not live* f *by the four elements alone* (Matthew 4:4). The gross food which enters the mouth does not alone sustain life, but the inward power and virtue—namely, the quintessence or fifth essence—in which the tincture lies as a spiritual fire.

<margin>g Works or effects.</margin>
24. The elementary food g produces only mortal flesh and provides a source or movement for the mortal life, but the spiritual man takes his nourishment from the tincture, for he himself is a tincture—namely, a spiritual fire. Therefore, Christ pours or overflows his heavenly flesh and blood—namely, the holy anointing oil—into the life of man, through and with the nourishment of the true life: that is, through the tincture of bread and wine. This is not to suggest that the tincture of bread and wine is able to grasp such a thing on its own, but it is only a permissive *medium* for it, just as the outward mouth of a man is only a *medium* through which the power and virtue of the tincture in the food is overflowed into the spiritual man. And because the highest tincture lies in bread and wine, which is nearest to the life of man and sustains the life of man most of all, Christ has therefore also ordained this testament under it.

No; if that were so, then bread and wine could comprehend the divine power and virtue in their own capacity, and Christ's

That same human and divine substantial word is given to man with the *tincture* of bread and wine, as through a *medium*, so that there may be a *visible* sign of what is done in the inward ground.

Christ has not *bound* himself to bread and wine *alone*, but has bound himself to *faith*, promising that he will be in man, he will continue in him, and man shall continue in Christ. His powerful living Word would continue substantially in faith; faith may always (as it always could) eat of this substance, for it is faith's nutriment, in which faith subsists and is a substance. Also, *Shall we who belong to Christ still be sinners? Far be it from us, for then Christ would be a servant of sin in us* (Galatians 2:17). This same substantial faith is also the branch on the vine of Christ, whose power and virtue penetrate through the whole man (as the sun does a herb) and kills the lusts and works of the flesh.

29. Mortal flesh is not substantial faith; that flesh does not comprehend Christ’s flesh and blood, but endures it as a rough stone endures the *tincture of gold*. And as fine *gold* lies and grows in the *rough stone*, so also the *love of Christ* grows in the *true life of man* and penetrates through that life, as fire does through iron. This is not because such power existed in man's life that it could receive the substance of God by its own power and virtue; no, it is given to him by grace, just as the *sun* of its own will gives itself to the *herb*. <margin>Note.</margin>
30. We are members of Christ according to his humanity, and are therefore called *Christians*, because Christ dwells and works in us, in that we are, in our life, his twigs and branches—those in whom he brings forth fruit through his power and virtue. Oh little children, it is a great mystery! Note, note: the use of this Testament—where we, especially under bread and wine, should partake of Christ's flesh and blood—is ordained so that we should come together and eat of one bread, drink of one cup, and thereunder receive Christ. We should *instruct* one another about what he has done for us, and declare his suffering, death, and blood-shedding *among one another*, and *teach it to our children*. We should bind ourselves with it in love, and exhort one another that we in Christ are members of one body—that we in Christ are all but one.

<image-desc>A hand-drawn ink circle or enclosure surrounds the entirety of paragraph 31.</image-desc>
31. Just as the one and only Christ gives himself to us *all* in common to be our only life, and loves us all in his one and only humanity, and reaches out that same only humanity with his great love and grace to us all in common, under one bread and wine, and so binds himself to us in one only participation: so also should we, in *such a coming together* and participation, as members of one body, bind ourselves together in true love and faithfulness. We should seriously consider that in *such* participation, we are all but one in *Christ*. For we all partake of the one and only Christ, and in that same one Christ we are one only body, which is Christ in his members. Oh little children, what a depth of mystery this is if we rightly considered it! Satan, in the anger of God, has rent us asunder and made us *at odds*, so that we have *opposite* thoughts. And here comes Christ with his love, and makes us all, in himself, one only man again, which is he himself in us; so that all of us together have become branches rooted in his tree, which he himself is; and we all live from his virtue and substance, and stand in one stock, which is himself.

32. Therefore, we should rightly consider this, and not with unworthy hearts and mouths draw near to this communion, and suppose it is enough that we partake of bread and wine. No, it is a brotherly, memberly bond and covenant; we bind ourselves with it in Christ to be one only man, and that same one only man is every person himself in Christ. Therefore, our purpose in *such coming together* should be that we, as members of one body, will bind ourselves fast with such participation, and forsake Satan with his

<summary>This page explains that Christians are branches of Christ's humanity, brought into unity through the communal partaking of bread and wine. The author emphasizes that the Eucharist is a covenant of love intended to reunite humanity into one body in Christ, overcoming the divisions caused by Satan.</summary>
<keywords>Christians, Mystery, Testament, Bread and Wine, Communion, Covenant, members of one body, unity, Satan, branches and twigs</keywords>

--- Page 128 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the spiritual nature of the Lord's Supper and begins a new chapter regarding the participation of the wicked in the sacrament.</meta>

his opposite will, and heartily love one another, *as Christ has loved us, and has given his life into death for us.*

33. To this end is this Testament of participation under bread and wine ordained: not to be understood as if it were a participation apart and separate, of which a Christian could not partake without the use of this ceremony. For if we are in Christ, and he himself is in us and is our life and light, and we are thus rooted in him in the substantial faith—which substance of faith he himself is—why then should that same life's desire not be able always to eat of it, if it only turns itself toward him? This ordinance is only a member-like bond of love, so that we might thereby instruct one another in what Christ has done for us, until he shall visibly come to us again in his assumed human image-likeness, and as our true High Priest be forever with us and in us.

---

### The Fourth Chapter.

## Of the difference of such participation; what the wicked receive by this Testament: and how a man should rightly prepare himself for it, so that he may be truly worthy.

->I.<-

As mentioned above, it does not lie in the conceit or supposition that one only needs to draw near with the body to such Communion and think: "When I have participated in the bread and wine with others, then I have received the true Testament of Christ, whereby my sins are forgiven me." No, *St. Paul* says: *He that distinguishes not the Body of the Lord from bread and wine, he receives it to his own judgment* <note>original: 1 Cor. 11:29. Modern translations often use "condemnation" or "judgment" for the Greek *krima*.</note>. Bread and wine cannot apprehend this Testament, much less the faithless, who come to it only out of custom and so that they may have the name of a Christian.

2. So also it does not stand in the priest's power, with his blessing, to bring the Body and Blood of Christ into the bread and wine; but it stands in the Divine Institution <note>The "Divine Institution" refers to the original act and command of Christ at the Last Supper, which gives the sacrament its power regardless of the priest's personal merit.</note>. This Institution is hidden from the wicked mouth, and it happens to such a person as it did to *Judas*, who, though he indeed also ate and drank of the bread and wine of the Supper;

<summary>This page argues that the Lord's Supper is a bond of love and a memorial of Christ's sacrifice, emphasizing that the sacrament's spiritual reality is accessed through faith rather than mere physical consumption or priestly ritual.</summary>
<keywords>Lord's Supper, Communion, faith, St. Paul, Judas, Divine Institution, High Priest, spiritual participation</keywords>

# Chapter 4. What the wicked receive in this Testament.

Supper; and although he was invited to the Testament, yet he did not receive Christ’s flesh and blood—that is, the Love of God. For after the Supper, *Satan entered into him* (John 13:27). This is as much as to say: the power and virtue of the Testament touched him, so that the mouth of his inward, false faith was stirred and opened. But as the mouth of his faith was, so was the Testament in his participation, as the Scripture says: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse.* <margin>Psalm 18:26, 27.</margin>

3. He received the Testament of Christ, but it was the Judgment only. In the holy or the saints, this Judgment kills the earthly will of the Serpent <note>The "Serpent" refers to the sinful, fallen nature of humanity inherited from the Fall of Adam.</note>; that is, when the soul is capable of receiving the holy power and virtue, so that it has a "mouth of faith" which receives the Love in the Testament, then it also receives *along with it* Christ’s suffering, death, and resurrection, which kills sin in both soul and flesh. But the wicked mouth is not capable of Love; therefore, it receives only Christ’s suffering and death, *and not his resurrection*. For with his false opinion, he lays his desire—the mouth of his false faith—to Christ’s flesh and blood, and *kills Christ* within himself through this participation in the Testament. He is thereby guilty of the death of Christ, for with his false participation and false desire of faith, he touches the severe judgment of God found in Christ’s agony, wounds, and death.

4. Therefore, because he is only capable of receiving the Judgment, the Judgment of God—which killed Christ for the sake of our sins—is stirred in the soul and character of the wicked person during this Testament. This Judgment touches or stirs Christ’s wounds and death in his Testament through this participation of a false nature. For the false "seed of the serpent" <note>original: *Serpents-seede*; a reference to the spiritual offspring of evil or the devil.</note> stings Christ in the heel with this touch <margin>Or, pierces.</margin>. Christ offers the soul his Testament and desires to slay the Serpent; but while Satan holds the chief dominion in the soul, the soul will not receive it. Instead, through the essence of the soul, it casts its false, poisonous rays against <margin>Or, against.</margin> the wounds of Christ and desires to kill Christ.

5. Understand this: he desires to poison and infect that ground within man where Christ’s blood, death, and resurrection should be possessed. This evil stirs itself all the more in the false soul, just as it did in *Judas*. When he received this Testament, then evil was stirred in *Judas* and took hold of his life. Therefore the Scripture says: *After the piece of bread, Satan entered into him* (John 13:27). Because his false heart had stirred the judgment of God, that judgment also entered into him; it was already *within* him beforehand, but not manifested until he touched the Covenant in the Testament. So it went with him as it did with *Uzza*, *who unworthily touched the Ark of God* (2 Samuel 6:6, 7; 1 Chronicles 13:9, 10) <note>Uzza was struck dead for touching the Ark of the Covenant, illustrating the danger of approaching the holy without proper reverence.</note>. A wicked man ought to take note of this: that he should not touch this Testament without repentance for his sins; otherwise, he lays...

<summary>This page explains that when a wicked person like Judas partakes in the Lord's Supper, they receive God's judgment rather than His grace, effectively "killing" Christ within themselves. The author warns that approaching the sacrament without repentance is spiritually dangerous, comparing it to Uzza's fatal mistake in touching the Ark of the Covenant.</summary>

places his hand of false possession on *Christ’s Wounds*, *Agony*, and *Death*, and will in the end receive *Judas’s* wages from it.

6. It is not to be understood as if the wicked, who joins with others in the Covenant of the Testaments of Christ, receives nothing; this is also to be understood regarding *Baptism*. For the Testament remains firm, and the Institution continues in power and virtue, because the *faithless* do not take away the Covenant and its power. The Covenant extends to all who participate and use it, but the participation depends on what the "mouth" is like. God’s great Love and Grace—namely, the *Substantial Love*—does not give itself into the wicked soul. Instead, the *Process of Christ*, with his *pain*, <margin>Or, Anguish.</margin> *Agony*, *Scorn*, and *Death*, does indeed enter the wicked soul. For in this, the *Man-Devil*—that is, the *wicked heart*—pierces Christ in his wounds and makes itself guilty of the *Death of Christ*.

7. The wicked indeed participate in *Christ’s descent into Hell*, in his *Anguish* and *Death*, but they are not capable of his *Resurrection*, in which Christ rules over *Death* and *Hell*. For their wicked will does not desire to die with Christ in *Christ’s Death* to their wickedness and false dealings and conduct, but instead desires only to arise and live with their sins in *Christ’s Spirit*. They want to rule in the *Resurrection* of Christ with their *earthly Lucifer*; therefore, they trample on the *Death* of Christ with the feet of their false desire, and do the same thing that the *Pharisees* did to Christ. Therefore, it would be better for such a person not to boast of being a *Christian*, and to not touch or meddle with *Christ’s Testament*.

## Concerning the Basis of Absolution.
### *What Forgiveness of Sins Is.*

->8.<-

<margin>Or, Ignorance.</margin>
<margin>Or, Sharp.</margin>
The false conceit and gross misunderstanding—when people teach that *Christ’s Testaments* destroy sin—needs a more *thorough explanation*, so that the wicked person may not hide himself under it, covering himself with *Christ’s purple robe* and wearing it as a *badge* of his falsehood. *Christ’s shedding of blood*, when he destroyed God’s anger with it and turned that anger into love, is what destroys sin. Whoever worthily participates in *Christ’s blood-shedding*, in him sin is destroyed through Christ's *victory* and *resurrection* with his *blood* and *Love*. Whoever comes to it with a *penitent heart* for past sins, and is angry at them, and has a strong purpose within himself *to enter into them no more*, he takes hold of the *Testamentary Grace* with faith.

9. It is false for someone to postpone their *repentance* until they participate in the *Testament of Christ*, [and then to think] that the same

True forgiveness and the destruction of sin require a penitent heart and a sincere commitment to change, rather than a superficial reliance on the sacraments.</summary>

will take away his sin: There is no forgiveness of sins, either by *Testament* or *Absolution*, unless a man converts from sin and is *renewed* through earnest, sincere repentance and turning into the grace of God in faith in Christ in the Holy Ghost: so that he takes up another will and resolution to go out from falsehood and wickedness.

10. For the forgiveness of sins in Christ's Testaments, and *without* the use of them, is nothing else but this: when Christ, in the converting sinner, arises from man's death into man's faith; and a new obedient will arises out of his death and becomes a light in man's life, so that the eternal night is turned into a clear day, then sin is forgiven. For if the eternal day of love dawns, then the night of the eternal darkness of God's anger is turned into love, and there the *Wedding of the Lamb* is rightly kept; and not with the unworthy heart, which, without repentance and forsaking of its sins, runs to the Testament and supposes that its sins are forgiven through the laying on of the priest's hand and participation in the Testaments.

11. The priest has *no* power to forgive sin; it does not stand in his own might and power. The power is in the ordinance of Christ: Christ in man, and so far as he is also in the priest himself, forgives sin to the repenting conscience. The absolution is but a *medium*, namely: an outward sign of it, so that we should receive one another in love and hearty forgiveness, and bind and reconcile ourselves in the love of the bonds of Christ, in his *Bride*, and in love receive one another into the *communion* of the body, namely: of the *Bride* of Christ.

<margin>Church.</margin>
<margin>Workingly.</margin>
12. The *minister* of Christ takes the faithful penitent man, through an outward absolution in Christ's stead, into the communion or congregation of Christ. He is, with his absolution, a *medium* or means of that which Christ himself, through his absolution, does *effectually* in the inward ground, and so man is through such a *medium* outwardly confirmed. But if there is no faith and conversion there, but only a custom, then there is also no absolution; for the priest's absolution without Christ's co-working is powerless and dead. For the power does not reside merely in the outward ordinance and in the priest, but in the communion of the saints in Christ, namely: in the Bride of Christ. This same [communion] takes the repenting man into their member-like fraternity in the *faith* of that which is to come; the Spirit of Christ in his members receives him.

13. A wicked priest, in whom the Spirit of Christ is not, *cannot* absolve him nor receive him; but the office of Christ, through the word of his promise, receives him. A false priest is but an outward, workless instrument as to himself, and does no more with this than

He clarifies that while priests act as ministers of absolution, the actual power to forgive and receive someone into the Church comes from Christ working within the community of believers.</summary>

the wicked priest with the *water-baptism*, who only pours the water and speaks the words without the co-working of the Spirit. But the Spirit of the Office looks not upon the unworthy minister of the office, but upon those who come to the office with faith. He <note>The Holy Spirit</note> absolves the believer through His office and receives him—along with the Bride of Christ <note>The Church</note> in whom He works—into the church or *congregation*. This is not done by a wicked *Pharisee* <note>original: "Pharisee"; here meaning a hypocritical or legalistic religious leader</note>, who himself is not capable of the office and only sits there as an *idol* which man worships. Such a man is himself but a devil full of falsehood and attributes to himself that which he *has not*.

14. There must be earnestness used in approaching such an office of the power of God, or else Christ is but mocked in it. Therefore, no man should rely solely upon the ordinance and think that the ceremony itself absolves him, or that he receives the testament merely for the sake of the ordinance and institution of Christ. If any man wishes to receive Christ into himself as a guest, he must then bring the temple of Christ—wherein Christ absolves him—with him into the ordinance. The absolution is but a receiving into the *communion* of the members of Christ; the priest receives him outwardly with the congregation, and Christ receives him in *faith* and binds Himself actively with him. This cannot be done without faith and an earnest turning toward God. And now, as Christ works inwardly in him, so also the congregation or *church* (namely, the Bride of Christ) works as fellow members in him through their prayers, and they all are but one in Christ.

<margin>Note.</margin>
15. But it is a snare of the Devil when the wicked think: "I am a sinner indeed, but I will eventually go to the office of Christ, to the communion of saints, *and will be absolved and receive Christ's testament so that my sins may be forgiven once; then I will sin anew once my old sin is done away*." This is what happens when many *begin to hang their heads a little and put on a show of piety*, and presently afterward return to their old customs, into all abominations and blasphemy. These people crucify Christ and <gloss>pierce him in his wounds</gloss>; it goes with such a person as it did with *Judas*, into whom Satan entered after he had eaten. It would be better if he stayed away from it altogether as long as he is not in earnest, if he thinks he is and remains a true Christian.

### 16.
Christianity is not a bare concept—that a man need only acknowledge with the mouth and believe that Christ died for us and has satisfied <note>original: "satisfied"; meaning Christ paid the debt for human sin</note>
