# Chapter 4. Of True Christianity.

[It is often wrongly assumed] that sin is satisfied for in such a way that a man needs only to give assent to the Gospel, and hold the history of what was done by Christ to be true; and that a man needs only and *barely* to go to his Testament, and there receive the grace which he left behind him, and comfort himself with it, and account it as a merit and *imputed grace* <note>original: "imputed Grace"; the theological concept that Christ's righteousness is credited to the believer's account</note>. It is not enough for a man to hear sermons preached, and to be baptized into Christ, and go to the [Lord's] Supper, so long as a man only keeps the custom. This by no means makes a Christian; it must be earnestness. No one is a Christian unless Christ *lives* and works in him, as Christ himself says: *Without me you can do nothing* (John 15:5). Also: *He that gathers not with me scatters* (Matthew 12:30).

17. A Christian must be one spirit in and with Christ, and will and work in the power of Christ. It is a *living, active*, working grace in a Christian; a continually burning fire, a *feeling power* and virtue. Although it is often covered with the lust of the flesh and the vanity of the world, yet it <margin>Glows.</margin> glimmers and burns in the heart like a fire, and reproves the flesh and the vain lust of untruth; it rejects the false way and does not desire it. That same *inward fire* is the *Spirit of Christ*, which without ceasing breaks the head of the serpent—namely, the lust of the flesh. The flesh has the will of this world, but that same kindled ground <note>The "ground" refers to the deepest essence or foundation of the soul.</note> has the *will of God*. If anyone is a Christian, then he will hate and be angry at the will of the flesh; he will be an enemy to his evil fleshly lust, and constantly complain and hold himself to be *unworthy*. He will continually, with the inward will of his soul, sink himself into the <margin>Note: Purest.</margin> purest grace, into God's mercy. He will *not say* of himself, "*I am a true Christian*," but will continually press with his desire into God's mercy and fly to grace, so that he might become a true Christian. In all his conduct, he will always account himself *too unworthy* of such grace, and in continual resigned humility, with weeping and prayer, only press into grace. His whole life will be a *continual repentance*, and he will evermore desire to take hold of grace, just as it has taken hold of him.

18. A true Christian is *affrighted* at sin when the lust of the flesh would work sin. Also, when he sees others work sin, then he *groans* within himself at it and wishes that such evil were not committed; it is an abomination in his eyes. He loves *truth* and *righteousness* and hates the false way. And although the earthly flesh often unawares, without any purpose of will, falls upon him with a sudden false lust and many times brings him to fall—as happened with David and Solomon and many saints, and is still done—yet such a man in whom the Spirit of Christ is does *not lie in sin*. Instead, the inward ground—namely, the incorporated grace in the Spirit of Christ—comes *quickly* with God's <margin>Strict.</margin> severe righteousness, in [divine] anger,

<summary>The author argues that true Christianity is not found in outward rituals or historical belief, but in a living, internal fire where Christ dwells and works within the soul. This internal grace leads the believer to constant repentance and a rejection of the sinful desires of the flesh.</summary>

<keywords>true Christianity, imputed grace, indwelling of Christ, inward fire, repentance, spiritual warfare, humility, grace vs. works</keywords>

## Of the worthy preparation to the holy
## Testament of Christ.

### 19.

If anyone will call himself a Christian, and comfort himself with the merits of Christ, and approach to his Testament <note>In this context, the "Testament" refers to the Sacrament of the Lord's Supper or Eucharist.</note>, and worthily receive the same, let him consider his ways well, and look to his heart, and see how it is framed: 1. Whether he stands in such a desire as to be *willing* to die wholly to vanity; 2. Whether it be in his purpose to *go out* from all falsehood, unrighteousness, lying, and deceit, and that he be resolved never to enter into them again; 3. Whether he desires the grace of God in Christ with a clean, pure will; 4. Also whether he is sorry for his sin; 5. Whether he finds and feels such a will in himself that from now on he will *wholly* go forth from his former sins and blasphemies; 6. And also whether he is so minded that he will yield up *his whole heart* and will to God’s mercy; 7. Also whether he finds, feels, and knows a *place* in himself where he will lay up this high Testament, namely: the flesh and blood of Christ with his grace; 8. Also whether or not he has made *room* in his heart and whole soul for the Spirit of Christ, that he may there enter in as a *living conqueror over death and hell*, and may erect *his kingly palace* in his heart and soul; 9. And whether he be *capable* of this, where Christ says: *We will come to you and make our dwelling in you*, John 14:23; 10. Also whether the temple of the Holy Spirit in him be swept and purged with right repentance; 11. Also whether there be a right *mouth* in him that can rightly receive Christ’s holy flesh; 12. Also whether the essence of his life be so framed that Christ with his substance and with his love may *continue* therein? For Christ says: *He that eats my flesh and drinks my blood continues in me and I in him*, John 6:56; 13. Also whether he finds in his mind that the stream of the living waters of *divine love* flows from him, that he loves his God, and his brother and neighbor as himself; 14. Also whether he wishes and desires to *do good to his enemies*; 15. Whether he accounts—

...counts anything in this world as *his own*, of which he says: *This is mine own only*.

16. Or whether in that which he has and possesses he accounts himself but a servant of God, and a steward to him and his brother in it: and consider that he is but an *officer and servant of God* in his state and condition, and in his temporal goods, that none of it is his own, but God’s and his brother’s? 17. Also whether he trusts God in his conduct, and keeps and esteems his *life* as his Lord Christ did; who was but a *pilgrim* in this world, and had nothing for his own, and also willingly left his *life* for his brethren? 18. Also whether he finds a sparkle of such a will in himself.

20. Now if he finds all this in himself, then he is rightly worthy and very fit for such a testamentary participation: but if not, and yet finds such a hunger in himself, that he would willingly be and will so, then he is *in the drawing of the Father to the grace in Christ*; then he should not long parley with reason, and frame doubts in himself, but should that very hour enter into such an *earnest purpose*, that he will enter into *earnest sincere repentance*, and continually fly to the *grace of God in Christ*, and pray that he will give him such a heart and will, and not attribute it to himself, as if he would attain it in his own power and virtue, but purely and merely sink into the most pure grace, into the mercy of God, and be in himself as a young child, which only has a desire for the *mother's milk*, that cannot help itself, but flies to the mother for her to help him.

21. His going to the *Communion of Saints* should be in *humility*: with a right reconciliation with all those whom he has wronged, or who have wronged him, and should forgive all his enemies, and wish the same to them which he desires for himself: with the fear of God, and a penitent heart, in a right *earnest purpose*, he may approach there, and bring no desire of his own to the *testament of Christ*, nor willing to apprehend and comprehend such grace by his own ability, but only sink himself down, and wholly give himself up into the grace as unworthy: and cast himself upon the grace, to do with him what it will; and not at all seek to desire the *Spirit of Divine Joy*—understand, as a possession—but give himself up to it, and sink down in the grace, that the same (*Spirit of grace*) may be in him how and when it will.

## Of the worthy preparation for the holy Testament of Christ.

### 19.

If anyone calls himself a Christian, and comforts himself with the merits of Christ, and approaches his Testament to worthily receive it, let him consider his ways well, and look to his heart, and see how it is framed: 1. Whether he stands in such a desire as to be *willing* to die completely to vanity; 2. Whether it is his purpose to *go out* from all falsehood, unrighteousness, lying, and deceit, and whether he is resolved never to enter into them again; 3. Whether he desires the grace of God in Christ with a clean, pure will; 4. Also whether he is sorry for his sin; 5. Whether he finds and feels such a will in himself that from now on he will *wholly* go forth from his former sins and blasphemies; 6. And also whether he is so minded that he will yield up *his whole heart* and will to God’s mercy; 7. Also whether he finds, feels, and knows a *place* in himself where he will lay up this high Testament, namely: the flesh and blood of Christ with his grace; 8. Also whether or not he has made *room* in his heart and whole soul for the Spirit of Christ, so that he may enter there as a *living conqueror* over death and hell, and may erect his kingly *palace* in his heart and soul; 9. And whether he is *capable* of this, where Christ says: *We will come to you and make our dwelling in you*, John 14:23; 10. Also whether the temple of the Holy Spirit in him is swept and purged with true repentance; 11. Also whether there is a right *mouth* in him that can rightly receive Christ’s holy flesh; 12. Also whether the essence of his life is so framed that Christ, with his substance and with his *love, may continue* in it? For Christ says: *He who eats my flesh and drinks my blood continues in me and I in him*, John 6:56; 13. Also whether he finds in his mind that the stream of the living waters of divine love flows from him, so that he loves his God, and his brother and neighbor as himself; 14. Also whether he wishes and desires to do *good* to his enemies; 15. Whether he ac-

...[considers] anything in this world as his *own*, of which he says: *This is my own only*: 16. Or whether in that which he has and possesses he accounts himself but a servant of God, and a steward to Him and his brother therein: and consider that he is but an officer and servant of God in his state and condition, and in his temporal goods, that none of it is his own, but God's and his brother's? 17. Also whether he trusts God in his conversation, and keeps and esteems his *life* as his Lord Christ did; who was but a *pilgrim* in this world, and had nothing for His own, and also willingly left His *life* for his brothers? 18. Also whether he finds a sparkle of such a will in himself.

20. Now if he finds all this in himself, then he is rightly worthy and very fit for such a testamentary participation: *but if not*, and yet finds such a *hunger* in himself, that he would willingly be and will so, then he is in the *drawing of the Father to the grace* in Christ; then he should not long parley with reason, and frame doubts in himself, but should that very hour enter into such an earnest purpose, that he will enter into earnest sincere repentance, and continually fly to the grace of God in Christ, and pray that He will give him such a *heart* and will, and not attribute to himself, as if he would attain it by his own power and virtue, but barely and merely sink into the most pure grace, into the mercy of God, and be in himself as a young child, who only has a *desire* for the mother's milk, who cannot help himself, but flies to the mother for her to help him.

21. His going to the Communion of Saints should be in *humility*: with a right reconciliation with all those whom he has wronged, or who have wronged him, and should forgive all his enemies, and wish the same to them which he desires for himself: with the fear of God, and a penitent heart, in a right earnest purpose, he may approach it, and bring no own desire to the Testament of Christ, nor willing to apprehend and comprehend such grace by his own ability, but only sink himself down, and wholly give himself up into the grace as unworthy; and cast himself upon the grace, to do with him what it will; and not at all will to desire the Spirit of *Divine Joy*—understand, as a property—but give himself up to it, and sink down in the grace, that the same (Spirit of grace) may be in him *how* and *when it will*.

# Of the worthy preparation to the Holy Testament. Ch.4.

come to you upon your call, since you have bidden us poor men to come and you will refresh us,
<margin>Matthew 11:28</margin>
Be it done unto me according as you say, as you will; I give up myself herewith wholly and altogether. Do with me, a poor unworthy man, according to your grace, as you will; I will eternally be your own. Break now my *will*, and govern it *with your will*; I can and am able to do nothing but *sink now wholly and altogether into your grace*.

23. Such a man who so wholly gives himself up to God, and continues standing in such resignation with his *will*, will in the end, when the grace moves in him, find and feel what grace and divine love is. When the divine fire is kindled in his *life*, then he will feel and taste what Christ in him is, and find quickly how he has become another man of other thoughts and will.

24. And then he is a Christian, when Christ's love-fire strives with God's anger in the conscience; and it will be set before that man in Christ's process in this world, that he must follow after *Christ* in his suffering, anguish, pain, scorn, and persecution, and must take the cross of Christ upon him and be conformable to *Christ’s image*. Inwardly, there is strife against sin and fleshly lusts that are in him, so that he despises himself and hates evil lusts; and outwardly he has contempt, scorn, trouble, and misery. Whereas the world accounts him as a stranger and a fool, where reason looks upon itself as foolish, and he himself—regarding the matters and conversation of this world—will be a fool and hates everything which his flesh loves. Where there is no one who flatters him, but all his good friends shun him and withdraw themselves, except only a *few* of the children of God who take notice of it, and whom God sends him for his comfort: then he may think that he, at that time with Christ, hangs on the cross, and he behaves himself so that he would willingly even die with Christ for the sake of professing the truth, in hope that he also, in Christ’s victory and overcoming, shall arise with Christ and live eternally in Christ.

25. This is a Christian and worthy for the communion of saints, who has entered into this process and converses therein. All others who go only out of custom, and account Christ’s testament as an outwardly imputed grace, and—as a work done believingly—appropriate it to themselves and will receive it as a gift, but will *not be reborn* and be another man of other thoughts and will, and keep the

### *The Fifth Chapter.*

## Of the Contention, Disputation, and Strife of the Learned about Christ's Testaments: what they effect through it, and what is to be held concerning it.

->1.<-

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'N' featuring a central cross or shield surrounded by symmetrical floral and foliage patterns.</image-desc>I will add this chapter not out of a desire to meddle with anyone’s good conscience, but for the comfort of the *simple* children of Christ, whom men so lead into error, and shut up in opinions, and seduce from true understanding through contention, making of Christ's Testaments a mere *den of murder*, and binding and shutting up the conscience in snares and bonds. To them I will explain what they should hold concerning the contentious disputation of those *learned* in reason, and what they establish by it.

2. Christ's Testaments are nothing else but a bond and covenant between God and man, a member-like union of the children of men, where God, through the humanity of Christ, has bound himself with man again after the fall, to be their loving God. Now, all those who—*without* this member-like bond and covenant of love—contend and dispute about conceits and opinions, about knowledge that goes into its own self without Christ, have no true understanding. For *none knows Christ but the Father only, and those to whom the Father will reveal him,* Matthew 11:27.

3. Now, if Christ is revealed to and in a man, then he has no contentious disputation or strife with any man about that knowledge and skill. Instead, he is lowly and accounts himself unworthy of any such knowledge; he despises no one for the sake of the *difference* of his gifts but is in love with all men, letting every man have his own. He gives his neighbor only his loving will and considers how he may be a *member* of Christ and of his brothers and sisters.

<meta>catchword: 4. But</meta>

<summary>This page warns that those who return to sin are unworthy of the Testament and begins a discussion on how academic disputes over theology often distract from the true, loving covenant of Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Testament, conscience, contention, covenant, Matthew 11:27, humility, divine revelation, spiritual union</keywords>

--- Page 140 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the disputes among the learned regarding Christ’s Testaments, following the author's stated intention to provide comfort to the simple-hearted who are confused by these debates.</meta>

<header>Of the Contention of the Learned Ch. 5.</header>

4. But when men *run into so many opinions, and strive, contend, and dispute about them, and despise one another for the sake of the letter, and scorn, and give them to the Devil:* the simple Christian is to know that in all such strife there is *no true understanding*, but mere pride and *Antichristian* <margin>a Matters.</margin> actions, or a miserable confounding of the words of Christ, where nothing arises from it but disunion, insecurity, and enmity; nothing else is achieved by it but the Devil’s will.

5. And that is because *they present themselves as teachers of the kingdom of Christ, and yet are not sent or known by God;* they take all their material from others, and exchange words and letters, and contend and dispute about the craft of how men may skillfully set letters together and form opinions—which composition is most artificial—and they force *consciences* into that *skill*; but they themselves *do not understand what they do:* their knowledge sticks purely to the *letters*, and yet they do not have the *Living word* CHRIST in them, who gives testimony to the *literal word*. Had they Christ in love within them, then they would impart that same love, and would show men Christ in themselves, and would so walk that *men* might see they were Christ’s children. But while they only contend and dispute, and will not so love and live themselves, it is false and but a hypocritical show.

6. To see that this is true, let men look upon the assumptions about which they contend: One group says, *Christ is substantially under the bread and wine:* also, *the bread and wine are changed into the Testament of Christ, and there is a change of the bread and wine;* and so they wish to receive Christ into the animal-like and mortal man—which animal nature, however, is not profitable <margin>b Text: In holiness.</margin> as to holiness. The other party says: *It is only a sign and symbol of the body of Christ, that he was broken or died for us;* and they deny the <margin>c All-present, presential.</margin> present substantial participation, and understand nothing concerning man—what and how he is, and what he lacks and needs so that he may attain the salvation of God again. The third party tries to handle the matter best and keeps to the words of the Institution, but says: *That Christ is participated with under the bread and wine; that is, Christ’s flesh and blood is eaten and drunk under the bread and wine;* but yet they have no understanding of how that comes to pass, or what is in man that is capable of such a gift, and will know nothing of the inward ground and right Adamic humanity; they also moreover deny the substantial inhabiting of Christ, and are as far from understanding as either of the other two. Neither will they know how the participation is wrought, but depend purely on the dead letter, yet they strike out, lash, and thunder with railing, scorn, heresy, and blasphemy.

<meta>catchword: 7. Every</meta>

<summary>The author critiques the prideful and divisive disputes among scholars regarding the nature of the Eucharist, arguing that their focus on the "dead letter" rather than the living presence of Christ leads to hypocrisy. He outlines three failing theological views on the presence of Christ in the bread and wine, noting that none truly understand the spiritual transformation of man.</summary>

<header>Ch. 5. About Christ's Testaments.</header>
<sig>Q</sig>
<meta>catchword: 11. Men</meta>

<margin>d Imaginary.</margin>
<margin>e Or, Masters and Dispensers.</margin>
<margin>f Venerated.</margin>
7. Every party strives only for a literal *image*, and wants to have Christ bound to their <sup>d</sup> image-like opinion, and wishes to be the *patron* of that same image. They do this on no other ground but to attain glory, honor, and high *esteem* by it. This may be known because they all despise and scorn one another, so that each of them may maintain his <sup>d</sup> image-like opinion and be called and esteemed <sup>e</sup> Lords of the Mysteries of Christ. Yet in the depth of their hearts, they respect nothing but the earthly *Lucifer*, with temporary honor and self-will, and desire to be <sup>f</sup> worshipped as Christ. Every party would have it so that men should worship their *image*, namely: their *framed literal* opinion.

8. With Christ's Testaments, they distinguish their *images* and make them a *sect* with them, crying out: "*Here is the Church of Christ; there is heresy and seduction!* Cling to me, *here is Christ!*" And with this, they embitter the hearts of kings and princes, as well as the minds of the *people*, so that for the sake of an image-like opinion, one brother despises, disgraces, slanders, and blasphemes another. He accounts him and cries out against him as devilish, and will raise wars and bloodshed, and the desolation of land and people, for the sake of such *idol-images*. All of these are a mere empty shell without fruit and belong to the fiery world for separation.

9. Men have brought it to such a pass that the people think they are saved if they only cling to the opinion <insert>of such men</insert> and allow it, whether they understand it or not, as long as they honor a sectarian name by it and hold the *patron* of their opinion to be right. So, when someone hears the name of that person, he cries: "*Yes, yes, that is right!*" and yet does not know what it is. Men have so completely blinded and seduced the simple that people look only at *men's names* and think Christ is in the opinion. And what is even worse, men so compel the people with power into such image-like opinions, and have so blinded men, that they even forsake body and goods for the sake of an opinion which they do not even understand at its foundation, and they persecute, hate, and kill one another for it.

10. Every one of them cries out that men will *take away* the true doctrine—namely, the true faith—*from them*, and yet they do not have it, nor is it in their opinion. Thus, the world is completely filled and blinded with opinions. Men suppose that if a man uses Christ's Testaments according to the opinion of his patron, then there is blessedness and salvation in them; otherwise, there can be no salvation. And whoever does not imagine according to the same opinion, and hang upon and cling to them, cannot be saved; also, he is no true member of the right Christendom.

<summary>This text criticizes how religious sects use the sacraments to create literal, dogmatic "images" or opinions to gain power and honor. It argues that these sectarian divisions lead to social bitterness, war, and the persecution of others, as people follow the names of men rather than the true spirit of Christ.</summary>
<keywords>sectarianism, literalism, Christ's Testaments, religious conflict, idolatry, false doctrine, Lucifer, spiritual pride, Christendom</keywords>

--- Page 142 ---
<meta>This page continues the critique of religious factions that prioritize dogmatic interpretations over true faith, arguing that such disputes lead to spiritual and physical violence, much like the idolatry of the Israelites in the wilderness.</meta>

# Of the Contention of the Learned Ch. 5.

11. Men contend so miserably about such image-like opinions that the churches are made into nothing else but mere houses of contentious disputation and spiritual dens of murder. This murder in the end leads to wars and bloodshed, and mere disgrace and dishonor is done to Christ for the sake of his holy gifts and testaments. In truth, nothing else is erected thereby, just as Israel danced about the *Golden Calf* and made an *idol-calf* to be their God, and rejoiced in their own handiwork—namely, in their *idol-image*—and lost their God. Therefore, the anger of God was also kindled against them and devoured them all in the wilderness, so that such idol and image servants could not come into the promised land.

12. Loving brethren, do not contend about opinions; all contentious disputation is an image of an idol. Concerning his omnipresence, Christ has told us of no opinion where he says, *He will be with us every day to the end of the world* (Matthew 28:20). He does not say, "in such or such an opinion," but where he signifies his presence, he says this: "*as the lightning goes forth and shines to its going down, so shall also the ever-enduring coming of the Son of Man be*" (Matthew 24:27). And he says: "*The kingdom of God is inwardly within you*" (Luke 17:21).

13. Christ's light and power rise up in his children in the inward ground and shine to them through the whole course of their life; and in that wellspring of light is the kingdom of God in man. Now, if he does not have this, let him contend and dispute as much as he will, yet he will bring it into him by no opinion. But if he has it, out of the same wellspring flow even streams of *love*; it needs no opinion. Let him only see to it that Christ's kingdom be born in him, that Christ be man in him; otherwise, he is no Christian, regardless of his opinion. He must stand as a *branch in the vine of Christ* (John 15). The opinion does not help him, but the true faith, which is active through the breaking forth of love and works good works (Galatians 5:6). If he does not have the works of love, then he also has no faith. Opinion creates no salvation but is *Babel*, a confusion of the tongues of the one only love. No one is a Christian unless he loves his neighbor and desires to do him good.

<margin>g Or, childlike.</margin>
14. The outwardly imputed grace, without the innate and filial grace, is all false. When Christ works in a man, then he is a Christian, and then the sufferings, merits, and satisfaction of Christ avail him. When he has him in himself as an advocate who has done it, so that he does it also in him and attracts his merits to himself, then is the kingdom of God in that *merit*; otherwise, all image-like opinions are false. No work pleases God except what he himself works in man through *his Spirit*; therefore, let us be the children of Christ, and not the children of images.

<meta>catchword: 15. Christ</meta>

<summary>The author warns that religious disputation over rigid opinions is a form of idolatry that obscures Christ's inward presence and the necessity of active love. True Christianity is defined by the indwelling of Christ's Spirit and the performance of good works, rather than adherence to external dogma.</summary>
<keywords>religious contention, idolatry, Golden Calf, omnipresence of Christ, inward ground, kingdom of God, Babel, filial grace, faith and works, spiritual transformation</keywords>

<header>Ch. 5. Concerning Christ's Testaments.</header>

15. Christ has imparted his kingdom to us in his Testaments; he who will receive it must be his child, otherwise there is no inheritance. To practice repentance is better than to desire to know much. If knowledge is not given by God to a man, then he will not <insert>be able to</insert> understand the ground of the divine secret mystery; but if it is given to him, he needs no image. To continue in the humility and simplicity of Christ, and to cling to his love and grace without despising anyone, is good Christianity. Everything whatsoever that speaks of itself is Babel; in Christ

Christ has imparted his kingdom to us in his testament; so whoever wishes to receive it must be his child in the inheritance. To practice repentance is better than to desire to know much. If God gives a person all knowledge, then they will find it best to understand the foundation <note>original: "Ground"; meaning the underlying basis or essential reality</note> of the divine mystery <note>original: "secret"</note> that will be given to them. He waits in his own house—which is the house of Christ—for the fullness of Christ, to cling to his love and grace without doubting; this is the mark of a good Christian. All which <unclear/> of <unclear/> is Christ; we are all members, and Christ is one. AMEN.
